# DOE Repo — Session Progress Tracker

## Purpose

This file is the running progress log for the `full_web_design` DOE repo. It records what has been worked on, what is finished, and what is left unfinished or needs follow-up across working sessions. Albert can read it at any time to see exactly where things stand — what is done, what is in progress, and what still needs a decision or action.

## How This File Is Maintained

The documenter sub-agent owns this file and updates it in three situations:

- (a) A step of a process is completed but further action is still required.
- (b) Work is left unfinished at the end of a session or between sessions.
- (c) At the end of each working session, to record a clean summary of what happened.

Every entry in the Progress Log must carry a timestamp in the format `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`. Entries are added at the top of the log (reverse-chronological). The Open / Unfinished Items checklist is updated whenever an item is resolved or a new one surfaces.

The reviewer sub-agent passed the script with one low-severity note (the `ADD CONSTRAINT` isn't `NOT VALID`, so it briefly locks the table — acceptable at this row count). `app/api/course-waitlist/route.ts` now accepts `booking_type`, `company` (required for team, `422 invalid_company` if missing), optional `last_name`, and `places` (2–20 for team, `422 invalid_places` otherwise); a repeat booking (same course + email) REPLACES the earlier row via upsert (keeps `created_at`, sets `updated_at`), and the route computes a running total of places booked for that course for the operator email only (known limit: a plain, unpaginated `select`, so it would undercount past 1000 rows for one course). `lib/mailer.ts`'s `sendWaitlistEmails()` now sends an operator email with subject "Neue"/"Aktualisierte Kurs-Buchungsanfrage", rows for last name, booking type, company, places, and the running total line, plus a visitor acknowledgment that names the place count and company for a team booking. `components/courses/WaitlistForm.tsx` gained the For myself/For my team toggle (an ARIA radiogroup) and the team fields with client-side validation mirroring the route; a reviewer sub-agent MEDIUM finding on the toggle (missing `aria-labelledby`, no roving `tabIndex`, no arrow-key handling) was fixed and verified — the radiogroup now has `aria-labelledby`, roving tabindex, and `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight`/`ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` all flip the selection and move focus. `lib/copy.ts` carries the owner-approved EN/DE copy (Q18) — no "cohort" anywhere, selling points are real delivery experience and bringing your team, German labels "Privat"/"Für mein Unternehmen" — and a `priceUnit` ("per participant"/"pro Person", Q17) now shown next to the price on both the course card and the detail page. Interest-based scheduling stays internal-only, with no fixed minimum and no course-date concept shown to visitors. `directives/relaunch_courses.md` was updated with a new "Booking flow" section, revised table-check/verification steps, and new Edge Cases covering all of the above. **Flagged, not done:** `sites/xoxocom/content/datenschutz.md` (the generic privacy-policy template) does not mention that a course booking collects name, company, email, and place count — an owner/legal decision, not a code fix. **Deployed to production**: Netlify deploy id `6abbdb820360238239c32c94`, live at https://www.xoxocom.net. **Verified live**: `/courses` and the course detail page both 200; the new EN+DE copy is present (title, body, toggle, "per participant"/"pro Person" shown next to the price on both the card and the detail page), with zero "cohort"/"ersten Gruppe" occurrences; the API correctly rejects a team booking with no company (`invalid_company`) and rejects both 50 places and 1 place for a team booking (`invalid_places`); the table still holds exactly 1 row, confirming the verification probes wrote nothing. The running-total select's 1000-row cap (noted above) was flagged again post-deploy and intentionally left unfixed at current volume.

**Prior (2026-09-29, earlier): a design ("grilling") session started on course-booking changes requested by the owner's employer — no code changed yet, decisions only.** A new file, `sites/xoxocom/CONTEXT.md`, was added as a domain glossary defining terms for this feature: **Booking** (a course signup request, not a payment or seat reservation — always confirmed manually by the operator), **For myself**, **For my team**, and **Contact person**. Settled so far: booking stays a request (no payment, no seat reservation, confirmed manually as today); a **"For my team"** booking is made by ONE contact person who books several places for their team in a single booking — team members do not book themselves, so no per-member grouping or team records are needed; the form needs a visible **"For myself" / "For my team"** choice, and that choice must be recorded as a marker in the stored data and in the notification email; there is no self-service booking management (no edit/cancel by the visitor); the employer dislikes the word "cohort" because courses are scheduled based on expressed interest, not fixed cohorts, so that term will not appear in the shipped copy. Relevant existing fact confirmed during this session: `leads.course_waitlist` (created by `execution/add_course_waitlist_table.py`) already has unused `company` and `last_name` columns, and its unique index is `(course_slug, email)`. `sites/xoxocom/CONTEXT.md` was updated further with **Place** and an internal-only note on **interest-based scheduling** (a course gets a start date once enough places are booked, e.g. ~7 — this is an internal operating rule, not something visitors are told).

**Owner answers received, now settled:** no login/self-service accounts for now (Q7); visitors must never learn that courses are scheduled based on interest — the "course gets a start date once enough places are booked" rule is internal only, copy must read as normal attractive marketing, the word "cohort" is dropped, and no "course date" concept is exposed to visitors (Q8 — this also means the earlier "Course date"/"Kurstermin" replacement-term recommendation is now moot, since that concept isn't shown at all); German toggle labels are "Privat" / "Für mein Unternehmen" (English stays "For myself" / "For my team") (Q9); a "for my team" booking asks for 2–20 places (Q10); a team booking requires a company name, treats last name as optional, and collects no individual participant names (Q11); a repeat booking from the same email for the same course replaces the earlier booking, and the operator's notification email for that case reads as an "updated booking" (Q12).

**Owner answers on Q13–Q16 received, now settled:** the interest threshold counts PLACES, not bookings (Q13); the "~7" figure used earlier was only an illustrative example, not a fixed minimum, so no threshold number needs storing anywhere (Q13, refines the earlier note); the operator notification email shows a running total of places booked so far for that course (Q14); the visitor-facing timing promise is "we'll confirm your booking, the start date and next steps by email" (Q15, supersedes the earlier "confirm your place and start date" wording); the selling points are "taught from real delivery experience" and "bring your team" — no "small groups" or "limited places" claims of any kind (Q16, drops the earlier conditional "small groups" recommendation entirely). `sites/xoxocom/CONTEXT.md` was updated to match.

**Prior (2026-09-20, courses session): a new Courses surface (index + detail page + waiting-list signup) shipped on `sites/xoxocom`, backed by a new Supabase table `leads.course_waitlist` — BUILT, VERIFIED LOCALLY, NOT DEPLOYED.** New script `execution/add_course_waitlist_table.py` created and ran successfully against the live shared-`.env` Supabase project (idempotent, `--check` supported); RLS is enabled with no policies, so only the server-side service-role key can read or write the waitlist. New site files: `lib/courses.ts`, the `components/courses/*` family, `components/content/CoursesContent.tsx`/`CourseDetailContent.tsx`, `app/courses/page.tsx`, `app/courses/[slug]/page.tsx`, `app/api/course-waitlist/route.ts`, 7 SVG logos under `public/course-logos/`; modified `lib/copy.ts` (new `courses` branch + nav entry, both languages), `lib/mailer.ts` (`sendWaitlistEmails()`), `app/globals.css`, `app/sitemap.ts`. One course is live in the catalog — `rag-agent-n8n-pinecone`, €1,099, "Coming soon" — with its headline/subheadline/highlights explicitly marked as draft editorial copy pending sign-off. **Three bugs found and fixed, all durable lessons**: (1) a unique index on `(course_slug, lower(email))` cannot satisfy PostgREST's `ON CONFLICT (course_slug, email)` — fails with Postgres `42P10` — fixed with a plain composite unique index plus a `CHECK (email = lower(email))` constraint; (2) awaiting the Gmail SMTP send before responding turned an already-committed row into a visitor-facing spinner and likely resubmit — fixed with an 8-second `Promise.race` time-box in the API route (the sibling contact-form route, `sites/_template/app/api/submit/route.ts`, still has this exact unbounded-await shape and was deliberately left alone this session); (3) a `justify-center` scroll rail parks off-zero once content overflows, cutting off the first card even on first paint — fixed by snapping `scrollLeft` to 0 on the overflow transition and matching `scroll-px-*` to `px-*`. Verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean, all routes/sitemap/404s confirmed, a full duplicate-signup/validation/i18n/mobile pass done end-to-end against the real Supabase table, all test rows then deleted (table is empty). `directives/capture_contact_submission.md` was corrected to stop calling the contact-form email send "non-blocking" on response latency (it is only non-blocking to error handling) and gained a cross-reference to this session's time-boxing fix. **Update (same session, later): the reviewer sub-agent pass on the new script and API route has now completed and its findings were applied and verified** — `add_course_waitlist_table.py` now wraps its DDL execution in `try/except psycopg2.Error` (a raw traceback on migration failure became a diagnostic `migration FAILED and was rolled back: <error>` + exit 1) and gained a `lang IS NULL OR lang IN ('de','en')` CHECK constraint (both in `CREATE TABLE` and as an idempotent `ALTER` DO-block, since a CREATE-TABLE-only CHECK never lands on an already-existing table on re-run); re-verified end-to-end including rejected mixed-case-email and `lang='fr'` inserts and a clean real signup. **Update (same session, third pass): the owner reviewed the local preview and gave visual feedback on the course card artwork** — the "n8n × Pinecone" caption looked like an AI watermark and the SVG connector lines didn't actually meet the logo tiles. `components/courses/CourseArtwork.tsx` was rewritten: the caption is gone; tile positions and connector endpoints are now both derived from one `HUBS`/`CHIPS` source-of-truth table (previously two independently hand-written coordinate sets that disagreed); a new `edgePoint()` helper computes exact tile-boundary crossings and tucks the line end just inside the tile so it reads as a real join; tile backgrounds became fully opaque to hide the tucked-in ends; the `float-drift` chip animation was removed (a drifting tile is a tile its connector no longer meets); the OpenAI chip was repositioned to clear both the card's arrow badge and the Pinecone hub. Verified clean at three viewport sizes. **Both items noted here as open are now RESOLVED (2026-09-20, fourth pass, same session): the owner approved the build, it was deployed to Netlify production, and the draft editorial copy is now live and downgraded to an optional revision rather than a blocker** — see the new "Latest" paragraph above and the 2026-09-20 (fourth pass) Progress Log entry. See the 2026-09-20 19:45 Progress Log entry and its three same-session follow-ups for the full build breakdown.

Typecheck is clean, and a real production build (`serve_prod.py up --slug xoxocom`) served all twelve routes at 200, with the Supabase-outage degrade path exercised for real (14 `PGRST106` errors logged, zero user-facing failures) since the `blog` schema is still not exposed. **The soft-404 defect is now RESOLVED (later the same session).** Root cause: `app/blog/loading.tsx` and `app/blog/[slug]/loading.tsx` each put their segment (and, for the first file, its inherited child segment `/blog/[slug]`) inside a Next.js Suspense boundary; once that fallback streams, the HTTP status is locked at 200 before a later `notFound()` can set 404 — documented Next.js streaming behavior, repeatedly reported and closed-without-fix upstream (`vercel/next.js` #45801, #64446, #70447, #76474), not a bug in this codebase. The earlier working hypothesis (root layout's `cookies()`/`headers()` calls) was wrong. Fix: both `loading.tsx` files were deleted; the index skeleton was preserved via a `<Suspense>` inside `app/blog/page.tsx` wrapping `<BlogIndexContent>`, with its fallback moved to a new `components/blog/BlogIndexSkeleton.tsx` — a `<Suspense>` inside a page is scoped to that route only, and `/blog` never calls `notFound()`, so nothing is lost. The article route now ships no loading skeleton at all, a deliberate trade for a correct 404. Verified on the dev server post-fix: `/blog/<unknown>` 404; `/blog` 200 with one `<h1>` and real content, not stuck on the skeleton; `/blog?tags=x` 200; homepage and legal pages 200; sitemap 200 with 11 URLs; typecheck clean. **The fix was then also confirmed against a real production build** (`serve_prod.py up --slug xoxocom`): `/blog/<unknown-slug>` returned a real 404 with `noindex` present, all twelve public routes returned 200 (`/`, `/produkte`, `/ueber-uns`, `/kontakt`, `/impressum`, `/datenschutz`, `/agb`, `/leistungen/ai-transformation`, `/blog`, `/blog?tags=x`, `/sitemap.xml`, `/robots.txt`), the sitemap had 11 URLs, and `/blog` rendered exactly one `<h1>`; the production server was then stopped (`serve_prod.py down --slug xoxocom`). The soft-404 item is now fully closed on both dev and production — no verification is carried into the next session. `directives/blog_publishing.md` was updated with the resolved root cause and a standing rule: never add a `loading.tsx` under `app/blog/` or `app/blog/[slug]/` — loading UI for the index belongs in a `<Suspense>` inside `app/blog/page.tsx`, matching a regression-guard comment now in that file. **Open now:** `blog.authors` is still empty, so nobody can write yet. Same day, later still: the Open Graph share image and site tagline — the last remaining German-language surface, missed by the earlier flip because it lives in `app/opengraph-image.tsx` and `site.config.json` rather than the copy/i18n system — were translated to English (headline, subtitle, `tagline`, plus the config `language` field). Committed as `c818d32` ("feat(xoxocom): translate Open Graph share image + tagline to English") and deployed to production via the same OneDrive external-build workaround (deployId `6a5a256c964ee67a377e2cc2`); the live OG image, JSON-LD description, and SEO score (100/passed) were all re-verified against https://www.xoxocom.net. `auto_optimize_seo.md` was checked and found not to describe the OG image's text/language, so no directive edit was needed. Same day, later still: the user reported the share preview STILL looked German after that deploy, and that WhatsApp's preview differed by device (English desktop, German mobile) and showed a small thumbnail instead of the large banner. Diagnosis: the origin was already serving the correct English OG image — the German the user saw was a stale cache, because `app/opengraph-image.tsx` is served at a stable URL with a one-year immutable `Cache-Control` header, so browsers/CDN/scrapers that had already fetched the old German image never see content changes at that same URL. Two fixes, both committed and deployed: `cfad68d` versioned the OG image URL (`OG_IMAGE` in `lib/seo.ts` → `"/opengraph-image?v=2"`) so every cache treats it as new and refetches (deployId `6a5a113578e0009681250c`); `02b0d92` added `type: "image/png"` to the OG images object (both `lib/seo.ts` and `app/layout.tsx`), emitting `og:image:type` to strengthen the large-preview signal scrapers use, fixing the small-thumbnail symptom (deployId `6a5a7e3143be8e068db17eca`). SEO stayed 100/passed after both deploys. The remaining "German on WhatsApp mobile" report is WhatsApp's own per-device link-preview cache — keyed per shared URL, no server-side purge available, self-clears over days or immediately with a new query string or a forced re-scrape via the Facebook Sharing Debugger — and is expected, not a code defect. `directives/auto_optimize_seo.md` was updated with a new Edge Case documenting the immutable-cache/versioning requirement and the WhatsApp caveat.

## Progress Log

### 2026-09-29 (later) — "For myself" / "For my team" course booking BUILT, REVIEWED, and DEPLOYED to production

Q17 and Q18 (the two open questions carried from the earlier same-day design session) are now resolved and implemented, closing out the course-booking feature described in `sites/xoxocom/CONTEXT.md`.

**Database.** `execution/add_course_waitlist_table.py` gained three new columns on `leads.course_waitlist` — `booking_type text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'self'`, `places integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, `updated_at timestamptz` — plus a `course_waitlist_booking_shape` CHECK constraint enforcing that a `self` row has exactly 1 place and a `team` row has 2–20 places and a non-null company. All additive (`ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, idempotent constraint guard), so the migration ran clean against production Supabase today; the table's one existing row picked up the defaults and is now `self`/1, matching what it always was. The reviewer sub-agent passed the script with one LOW-severity note: the `ADD CONSTRAINT` is not `NOT VALID`, so it briefly locks the table while validating existing rows — acceptable at the current row count, worth revisiting if the table grows large before a future ALTER.

**API route.** `app/api/course-waitlist/route.ts` now reads `booking_type` from the posted fields (anything other than `"team"` is treated as `self`); a `team` booking requires `company` (`422 invalid_company` if blank) and a `places` value between 2 and 20 inclusive (`422 invalid_places` otherwise), with `last_name` optional. A repeat booking for the same `(course_slug, email)` REPLACES the earlier row via the existing upsert — `created_at` is preserved, `updated_at` is stamped, and the operator notification's subject/opening line switch from "Neue"/"new" to "Aktualisierte"/"updated" so the operator can tell the two cases apart. The route also computes a running total of places booked for that course (`SELECT places WHERE course_slug = ...`, summed in JS) and passes it to the operator email only — a known limitation, not yet worth fixing: the select is unpaginated and would undercount past 1000 rows for a single course, and a failed count doesn't block the booking, it just omits the total line.

**Email.** `lib/mailer.ts`'s `sendWaitlistEmails()` operator email now shows last name, booking type ("Privat"/"Für mein Unternehmen"), company, places, and — when available — "Bisher gebuchte Plätze für diesen Kurs: N". The visitor acknowledgment names the place count and company for a team booking.

**Form.** `components/courses/WaitlistForm.tsx` gained a "For myself"/"For my team" toggle and, when team is selected, company/last-name/places fields with client-side validation mirroring the route (a convenience only — the server re-validates independently). The toggle is built as an ARIA `radiogroup`; a reviewer sub-agent MEDIUM finding on it (missing `aria-labelledby`, no roving `tabIndex`, no arrow-key support) was fixed and verified before deploy — the radiogroup now has `aria-labelledby="wl-booking-for"`, `tabIndex={0}` on the checked option only, and `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight`/`ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` flip the selection and move focus to it.

**Copy.** `lib/copy.ts` carries the owner-approved EN/DE strings for the toggle, team fields, and both new error messages (no "cohort" anywhere, per the standing instruction from the earlier design session); the selling points read "taught from real delivery experience" and "bring your team," with no "small groups"/"limited places" claims. A new `priceUnit` string ("per participant"/"pro Person") is now shown next to the price on both `CourseCard.tsx` and `CourseDetailContent.tsx`, resolving Q17 — the price is per participant, not per booking.

**Directive.** `directives/relaunch_courses.md` gained a new "Booking flow" section documenting all of the above (types, validation, replace-on-repeat, running total, table columns, price-per-participant), an updated table-verification step (step 3) covering the new columns and constraint, an updated production-verification step (step 7) that now also exercises a team booking and a repeat-booking replace, two new Outputs bullets, five new Edge Cases (invalid team submission, groups over 20, running-total undercount/failure, no self-service edit/cancel, and the privacy-policy gap below), and a new Error Handling-adjacent privacy note.

**Deployed and verified.** Netlify deploy id `6abbdb820360238239c32c94`, live at https://www.xoxocom.net. Confirmed in production: `/courses` and the course detail page both return 200; the new EN+DE copy (title, body, toggle labels, "per participant"/"pro Person" next to the price on both card and detail page) is present, with zero "cohort"/"ersten Gruppe" occurrences; the live API correctly returns `invalid_company` for a team booking with no company, and `invalid_places` for both 50 places and 1 place on a team booking; `leads.course_waitlist` still holds exactly 1 row, confirming none of the verification probes wrote a row.

**Flagged, not fixed this session:** `sites/xoxocom/content/datenschutz.md` (the generic privacy-policy template) does not mention that a course booking collects name, company, email, and place count — this needs an owner/legal decision on wording, not a code change.

**Still open, carried forward:** the owner's own live test booking of both types through the production form (one "For myself", one "For my team", including a repeat submission to see the replace behavior), followed by deleting the test row(s) from `leads.course_waitlist`; committing and pushing today's changes to git (working tree is modified but uncommitted as of this entry, on top of the already-unpushed header-lockup commits from 2026-09-28); the running-total select's 1000-row cap, if the table ever approaches that size; and the standing unpushed-commit backlog noted in earlier entries.

### 2026-09-29 — Course-booking design session with the owner's employer: domain glossary added, no code changed, decisions pending

A design ("grilling") session covered course-booking changes the owner's employer wants. No code under `sites/xoxocom/app`, `lib/`, `components/`, or `execution/` was touched. One new file, `sites/xoxocom/CONTEXT.md`, was added as a plain-language domain glossary defining **Booking** (a signup request only — no payment, no seat reservation, always confirmed manually by the operator, same as today's waitlist/booking-request flow), **For myself**, **For my team**, and **Contact person**.

Decisions settled in-session: booking remains a request, not a transaction — no change to the "no payment taken" framing already live per `directives/relaunch_courses.md`; a **"For my team"** booking is made by ONE contact person who books several places for their team in a single booking — team members do not book themselves, so no per-member grouping or team records are needed (this drops the earlier open question about how team members would be grouped); the form needs a visible choice between "For myself" and "For my team," and whichever is chosen must be recorded both in the stored record and surfaced in the operator notification email; there is no self-service booking management (no visitor-facing edit or cancel); the employer specifically dislikes the word "cohort" in course copy, because courses are scheduled based on expressed interest rather than run as fixed cohorts.

One existing-schema fact was confirmed relevant: `leads.course_waitlist` (created by `execution/add_course_waitlist_table.py`, still governed by `directives/relaunch_courses.md`) already has unused `company` and `last_name` columns, and its unique index is `(course_slug, email)` — both may matter to how a "for my team" booking is stored (participant count, company name), but nothing in the schema or scripts was changed this session.

**Open, blocking copy drafting:** whether to add a number-of-participants field for a "for my team" booking, expressed as a range rather than an exact count; whether to add a company-name field; whether a repeat booking by the same contact person for the same course should replace their earlier booking or create a new one; confirmation that no login/account system is wanted (recommendation: correct, none needed); the replacement term for "cohort" (recommended: "Course date" / German "Kurstermin"); the exact German toggle labels ("Für mich" / "Für mein Team"). Once the owner answers, the planned next steps are: draft the toggle/field copy for approval, then implement — a form toggle plus the settled fields, a new booking-type marker on `leads.course_waitlist` (or an equivalent schema change), `lib/mailer.ts` notification updates, `lib/copy.ts` updates, a `directives/relaunch_courses.md` (or new directive) update describing the booking-type flow, a reviewer sub-agent pass on any touched execution/route script, and a deploy.

Also still open, carried from 2026-09-28: the owner's live end-to-end test signup through the production course form (`directives/relaunch_courses.md` step 7, test row to be deleted afterward); committing 2026-09-28's uncommitted courses-relaunch and socials-emptied changes to git.

### 2026-09-28 (final, later than the header-lockup entry below) — Courses RELAUNCHED with booking-request copy; socials removed from Footer/SEO; not yet committed

**Courses relaunched.** `sites/xoxocom/lib/features.ts`'s `COURSES_ENABLED` was set back to `true` — `/courses` and `/courses/<slug>` are live again, per `directives/relaunch_courses.md`. The owner settled that directive's step 1 open question by choosing option (a "book now" match, reframed as a booking request) rather than building a real payment/booking flow: the CTA and all surrounding copy now consistently describe sending a booking request, and the copy is explicit that no payment is taken. The form still only inserts a signup row into `leads.course_waitlist` via `/api/course-waitlist` — nothing was built to actually process payment or confirm a booking automatically.

**Copy changes in `lib/copy.ts`'s `courses` tree (both languages), owner-approved:** `comingSoon` badge "Coming soon"/"Demnächst" → "Now booking"/"Jetzt buchbar"; waitlist `eyebrow` → "Book your place"/"Platz buchen"; `title` → "Secure your place in the first cohort"/"Sichere dir deinen Platz in der ersten Gruppe"; `body` → "The first cohort is kept small. Send your booking request and we'll get back to you by email to confirm your place, the start date and the next steps. No payment is taken here." (DE equivalent, "Hier wird nichts bezahlt."); `submit` button → "Book now"/"Jetzt buchen" (the owner first tried "Book the course now"/"Kurs jetzt buchen", then shortened it); `successTitle`/`successBody` → "Booking request received."/"We'll email you shortly to confirm your place and the start date." (DE: "Buchungsanfrage erhalten."). ### 2026-09-28 — XoXoCom Courses pulled from public view (parked, not deleted); new `relaunch_courses.md` directive

**Trigger: the owner asked to pull the Courses section from public view because there are no courses on offer yet, while making clear it must not be deleted — they want to be able to say "push it back to Netlify" later and have it just work.**

**Mechanism — a single feature flag, not a teardown:** a new module, `sites/xoxocom/lib/features.ts`, exports `COURSES_ENABLED = false`. It lives in its own file rather than in `lib/courses.ts` because `lib/copy.ts` needs to read it for the nav item and `lib/courses.ts` imports `lib/copy.ts` — putting the flag in `courses.ts` would create a circular import. With the flag off: `lib/copy.ts`'s nav array omits the "Kurse"/"Courses" entry (both languages, via a spread conditional); `app/sitemap.ts` omits both the `/courses` URL and every `/courses/<slug>` URL; `app/courses/page.tsx` and `app/courses/[slug]/page.tsx` call `notFound()` in both `generateMetadata` and the page component itself; `app/api/course-waitlist/route.ts`'s `POST` handler returns a 404 JSON body before touching the database.

**Nothing course-related was deleted.** All of the following stay in the repo exactly as built in the 2026-09-20 Courses session: `app/courses/**`, `components/courses/*`, `components/content/CoursesContent.tsx` and `CourseDetailContent.tsx`, `lib/courses.ts`, the full `courses` copy tree (both languages) in `lib/copy.ts`, `sendWaitlistEmails()` in `lib/mailer.ts`, the SVG logos under `public/course-logos/` and `assets/course-logos/`, `execution/add_course_waitlist_table.py`, and the `.dash-flow` keyframes in `app/globals.css`. All of this remains untracked/uncommitted in git as of this session — see the Open Items list.

**One copy change was made per the owner's explicit request, and it was scoped deliberately narrowly:** the waitlist CTA string (`courses.waitlist.submit`), shared by both the course detail page's hero anchor button and the waitlist form's own submit button, was changed from "Join the waitlist" to "Book the course now" (EN) and from "Auf die Warteliste" to "Kurs jetzt buchen" (DE). Nothing else in the waitlist copy was touched: the eyebrow ("Waitlist"/"Warteliste"), the section title ("Be first in line when the doors open"), the body text, the success message, and both languages' course `metaDescription`s still describe joining a waitlist, and the underlying form (`/api/course-waitlist`) still only inserts a row into `leads.course_waitlist` — it does not take payment or create a real booking. This is a real content inconsistency between the new CTA and everything around it, deliberately left unresolved rather than silently patched over with invented copy; it is called out as a required pre-relaunch decision in the new directive, and as an Open Item below.

### 2026-09-20 22:15 — XoXoCom logo concepts F and G redrawn per owner feedback (sprint pose, right-to-left); Mark Study republished as Version 2

**Follow-up to, and continuation of, the 2026-09-20 21:00 entry below (same session, same seven concepts, same artifact).** No `execution/` script was touched this pass — the marks are inline SVG geometry inside the Artifact's own HTML, not `execution/build_logo_lab.py`.

**Why:** the owner reviewed the Mark Study and approved most concepts but rejected F (Sprinting Bot) and G (Bot to Stardust) on two grounds — the poses read as walking, not sprinting, and both marks travelled left to right when the owner wanted right to left, with more dynamism, and G's stardust trailing off to the right, behind the runner, rather than ahead of it.

**Fix, not just a mirror:** simply flipping the existing artwork would not have produced a sprint — the old pose had the torso and the lead thigh at nearly the same angle, so they merged into one shape and the legs never opened into a readable stride. Both `MARKS["F"]` and `MARKS["G"]` were redrawn from scratch with the torso pitched forward over the lead leg (head tilted to match, rotated -12deg), the lead leg reaching out ahead toward the ground, the trail heel kicked up above knee height (the detail that reads as a sprint rather than a walk), and both arms bent hard — one driving toward the chin, one swept back past the hip. F's speed lines now trail off the back of the head; G's trailing leg and rear arm break up with stepped opacity, and six four-point stars scatter to the right, shrinking in size and opacity. ViewBoxes changed (F: `7 0 103 104`, G: `7 0 106 104`); optical-weight multipliers adjusted (F 1.06, G 1.04).

**Copy followed the redraw:** F's written description, which still described the old walking pose, was corrected to match the new artwork. G's "which way does it dissolve" open question is now SETTLED — right to left, dust trailing behind — so that decision card was replaced with the consequence it creates: with direction locked, in a horizontal lockup the bot now runs *away* from a wordmark placed to its left; putting the mark to the *right* of the name instead would have it run toward the reader. That is a new open question for the owner, not yet answered. The "Four things to settle" section lede was reworded to reflect that one of the four is now decided.

**Deliverable:** republished to the same URL — https://claude.ai/artifact/SwExppRsR68N3GQQNFsMEZ — now Version 2. Still private, still needs sharing from the page's own Share menu (unchanged open item).

**Still stale:** the older 10-artboard canvas artifact (https://claude.ai/artifact/8mWNSEx8rcMjdnu6YGDBWK) holds the original left-to-right F and G poses and is now doubly out of date on top of already being superseded as the presentation format.

**Technical note (harness, not code):** browser-harness lost the active tab twice mid-session to a concurrent session running a dev server on `localhost:3000` — screenshots came back showing the wrong page. The reliable pattern going forward: open a fresh tab with `new_tab()` and screenshot it within the same browser-harness invocation, not across separate calls.

### 2026-09-20 21:00 — XoXoCom logo concepts rebuilt as a single scrolling-page Artifact ("Mark Study"); this is now the artifact to present; earlier 16px finding corrected; no execution script touched

**Follow-up to, and partial supersession of, the 2026-09-20 15:40 entry below (same session, same seven concepts).** No `execution/` script was written or modified this pass either.

**Why this exists:** the user rejected the earlier 10-artboard pan/zoom "Design" canvas (https://claude.ai/artifact/8mWNSEx8rcMjdnu6YGDBWK) as a presentation format — a pan/zoom canvas of 10 artboards is not something you hand to an audience, and they explicitly did not want people hopping between multiple links. They asked for everything visible and comparable at a glance, preferably within ONE artifact.

**New deliverable — this is now the artifact to present, superseding the canvas:** a single scrolling HTML page Artifact, titled "XoXoCom Mark Study," at https://claude.ai/artifact/SwExppRsR68N3GQQNFsMEZ. **Status: private** — must be shared from the page's own Share menu before the audience can open it; this has not been done yet. Structure, top to bottom: a sticky chip bar (A–G plus Containers / In place / Decisions, all in-page anchors) → "The line-up" (all seven marks in one row, same colour and optical weight, each linking to its own panel) → a "Comparison" matrix (7 concepts × 6 treatments: bare / accent box / dark box / mono / 30px / 16px) → one uniform panel per concept (four specimens, description, lockups on dark and light, a scale ladder) → Containers & colour → In place (favicon ladder, browser tab, site header on dark and light, app tiles) → "Four things to settle" → a footer. Deliberately a single-theme dark design (a logo specimen must not flip ground under the viewer) — tokens: ground `#0a0d10`, raise `#13171c`, line `#272d35`, ink `#edeff2`, dim `#8e9299`, coral `#fb6b4c`, paper `#eaecef`. Type: Manrope (brand, carries the wordmark) plus IBM Plex Mono for labels, pixel sizes, and hex values. Implementation note: the seven marks are defined once as SVG `<symbol>` elements and instanced via `<use>`, so the page's 148 mark instances cost one definition each; the resulting page is ~66 KB.

**Correction to the 15:40 entry's 16px finding — that entry's "B, F and G do not hold at 16px" was an estimate made before anything was rendered, and it is wrong. After actually rendering and inspecting the page, the real finding is:** **C (Starburst X) is the only mark that survives 16px untouched.** A (X-Face Head) and D (Star Frame) survive only as silhouettes and shed interior detail — D's inner x disappears entirely. B, E, F, and G do not read at 16px at all. **So six of the seven concepts would need a reduced companion glyph if chosen as the primary mark, not three.** The correction has also been annotated inline on the 15:40 entry below so the log isn't self-contradicting.

**Also fixed after the render check:** the comparison matrix's failed-size cells were originally rendered in a faint coral that read as a rendering bug rather than as information. The matrix now encodes three distinct states — normal (holds), grey (simplifies, loses interior detail), tinted coral (breaks down) — matching the corrected 16px finding above.

Confirmed again that browser-harness can attach to the wrong tab when other sessions are active — a first screenshot returned the user's live production XoXoCom site instead of the local preview file; opening a fresh tab and reading back its target id resolved it (consistent with the existing "browser-harness Google pages quirks" memory note, generalised here to any multi-session attach, not just Google apps). ### 2026-09-20 19:45 — XoXoCom Courses surface + `leads.course_waitlist` built and verified locally; NOT deployed, awaiting the owner's review

**Built this session:** a new Courses surface on `sites/xoxocom` (a course index, a course detail page, and a waiting-list signup form) plus its Supabase capture backend. New execution script: `execution/add_course_waitlist_table.py` — creates `leads.course_waitlist` via a direct `psycopg2` connection over `SUPABASE_DB_URL`, the same DDL pattern as `add_campaign_slug_column.py`/`add_blog_schema.py`/`add_prospects_dedup_constraint.py`. Idempotent, supports `--check` (read-only report). Columns: `id, course_slug, email, first_name, last_name, company, lang, note, site_slug, user_agent, ip, created_at`. RLS is enabled with **no policies at all** — deny-by-default, so only the server-side service-role key can read or write; the browser's anon key cannot see who signed up. Uniqueness is a plain composite index on `(course_slug, email)` (`ux_course_waitlist_course_email`) plus a `CHECK (email = lower(email))` constraint, not an index on `lower(email)` — see the ON CONFLICT lesson below. **Run successfully against the live shared-`.env` Supabase project; the table exists and is verified**, and is currently empty (all test rows were deleted after verification).

New site files under `sites/xoxocom`: `lib/courses.ts` (course catalog + `listCourses`/`getCourse`, asserts at module load that the German and English catalogs agree on slug and length); `components/courses/LogoMark.tsx`, `CourseArtwork.tsx`, `CourseCard.tsx`, `CourseCarousel.tsx` (client), `CourseStack.tsx`, `WaitlistForm.tsx` (client); `components/content/CoursesContent.tsx`, `CourseDetailContent.tsx`; `app/courses/page.tsx`, `app/courses/[slug]/page.tsx`, `app/api/course-waitlist/route.ts`; `public/course-logos/*.svg` (7 logos copied from `assets/course-logos/`). Modified: `lib/copy.ts` (a `courses` branch in both language trees — index chrome, detail chrome, waitlist form strings, and a `catalog` array — plus a Courses/Kurse nav entry beside Blog); `lib/mailer.ts` (new `sendWaitlistEmails()` — operator notification in German, acknowledgment to the signup in their own site language); `app/globals.css` (`.dash-flow`/`.float-drift` keyframes for the course artwork); `app/sitemap.ts` (now appends `/courses` plus every `/courses/<slug>` from `COURSE_SLUGS`). The one course live in the catalog: slug `rag-agent-n8n-pinecone`, "Build Your Own RAG Agent with n8n and Pinecone", €1,099, Beginner to intermediate, marked "Coming soon" — the page's only conversion action is the waiting list. **The headline, subheadline, three highlights, and waitlist-section copy are marked as draft editorial copy pending the owner's sign-off**; only the title, price, and level came from the owner directly. `app/courses/[slug]/page.tsx` deliberately ships no `generateStaticParams` — `getCopy()` reads `cookies()`, forcing dynamic rendering, matching the existing `/blog/[slug]` pattern.

**Three bugs found and fixed this session — durable lessons:**
1. **ON CONFLICT cannot target an expression index.** A unique index on `(course_slug, lower(email))` looks equivalent to a plain composite index but is not: PostgREST sends `ON CONFLICT (course_slug, email)`, and Postgres can only infer a conflict target from an index over exactly those literal columns, so every signup failed with `42P10`. Fixed by indexing the plain columns and preserving case-insensitivity structurally instead, via a `CHECK (email = lower(email))` constraint that the API route's own lowercasing already satisfies. This generalizes to any future Supabase upsert in this repo — `leads.prospects`'s existing `(campaign_slug, email)` index was already plain columns, so `capture_contact_submission.md` needed no correction, but the lesson is now recorded here and in the new script's own docstring for the next table that needs one.
2. **Awaiting SMTP before responding turns a saved lead into a retry.** The `/api/course-waitlist` route originally awaited `sendWaitlistEmails()` (Gmail SMTP) before returning, so a visitor sat on a spinner — and could resubmit — after their row was already committed. Fixed with an 8-second time-box (`EMAIL_TIMEOUT_MS`) via `Promise.race`, with the rejection handler attached to the send promise separately so a rejection arriving after the timeout has already "won" the race can't become an unhandled rejection. **`sites/_template/app/api/submit/route.ts` (the contact form) still has this exact same unbounded-await shape** — deliberately left alone this session since the owner did not ask for it; see Open Items and the `capture_contact_submission.md` reconciliation below.
3. **A `justify-center` scroll rail parks its scroll position off-zero once content overflows**, cutting off the first card — including on first paint, before hydration removes the class. Fixed by snapping `scrollLeft` to 0 on the not-overflowing→overflowing transition, and matching `scroll-px-*` to `px-*` (a mismatch settles the snap at ~16–24px instead of 0).

**Verification done:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `/courses` 200, `/courses/rag-agent-n8n-pinecone` 200, `/courses/<unknown>` 404; `/sitemap.xml` 200 and contains both course URLs; an end-to-end submit through the real UI wrote the correct row to Supabase; a duplicate submit with different capitalisation collapsed to one row; invalid email → 422 `invalid_email`; unknown slug → 422 `unknown_course`; German toggle verified on both pages; no horizontal overflow at 390px; carousel arrows correctly hidden with one course, and appear (prev disabled at start) once the rail overflows. All test rows were deleted from `leads.course_waitlist` afterward — the table is empty.

**Directive reconciliation done this pass:** `directives/capture_contact_submission.md` — corrected step 6's "best-effort, non-blocking" description of the email send (it is non-blocking to error handling only; the response is still delayed by the full SMTP round trip because the send is awaited) and added an Edge Case + Error Handling cross-reference to the time-boxing fix proven in `/api/course-waitlist` this session, as a documented option if the same latency ever needs fixing on the contact form. `directives/build_website.md` and `directives/deploy_to_netlify.md` were reviewed and found accurate — nothing about the deploy path or the scaffold flow changed this session (nothing was deployed), and Courses is a bespoke, hand-authored addition to one already-live site rather than a change to the scaffold template.

**Status — NOT DEPLOYED, three items open:** (1) the owner has not yet reviewed the local preview at `http://localhost:3000/courses` — the standing rule is human review before any Netlify push, and nothing was pushed; (2) the draft editorial copy (headline, subheadline, three highlights, waitlist section copy) needs the owner's sign-off or rewrite; (3) a reviewer sub-agent pass was launched against `add_course_waitlist_table.py` and the `/api/course-waitlist` route but had not reported back by the end of the session — its findings still need to be applied before this is considered done. Optional follow-up, not yet actioned: apply the same SMTP time-box fix to `sites/_template/app/api/submit/route.ts`.

**Follow-up (later the same session) — reviewer pass on `execution/add_course_waitlist_table.py` + `app/api/course-waitlist/route.ts` COMPLETED, findings applied and verified; item (3) above is now resolved.** Confirmed clean, worth recording so it isn't re-litigated: the `%` characters inside the DDL (`ILIKE '%lower(email)%'`, `format('DROP INDEX leads.%I', idx)`) are safe because `cur.execute(DDL)` is called with no `vars` argument, so psycopg2 performs no %-substitution at all; `--check` is genuinely read-only (SELECTs then rolls back) with no reachable `UnboundLocalError` on `ok`; RLS deny-by-default plus service-role-only grants are correct; nothing sensitive (emails/IPs) is ever printed, only counts and column names; `onConflict: "course_slug,email"` matches the unique index; and the email-timeout `Promise.race` attaches `.catch()` to the send promise before racing it and always assigns `timer` synchronously, so there is no unhandled rejection or dangling timer. Two fixes applied to `execution/add_course_waitlist_table.py`: (a) MEDIUM — `cur.execute(DDL)` is now wrapped in `try/except psycopg2.Error`, matching the sibling `add_blog_schema.py`; a DDL failure now prints `migration FAILED and was rolled back: <error>` to stderr and exits 1, instead of a raw traceback (the `with conn:` block already guaranteed rollback — only the operator-facing message was missing); (b) LOW — `lang` had no DB-level constraint (unlike `blog.posts.lang`); added `CHECK (lang IS NULL OR lang IN ('de','en'))`, in both the `CREATE TABLE` and as an idempotent `ALTER ... ADD CONSTRAINT course_waitlist_lang_known` DO-block so it also lands on the already-existing table — a CHECK written only inside `CREATE TABLE ... IF NOT EXISTS` never applies on a re-run against a table that already exists, hence the separate ALTER. Also documented in-place (no code change) why `last_name`/`company` columns exist unused today: the launch form deliberately asks only for email + first name, and the columns make a longer form later an additive change rather than a migration. Re-verified end-to-end after the fixes: migration re-run cleanly (idempotent); `--check` reports both CHECK constraints present; a mixed-case email insert and a `lang='fr'` insert were both correctly rejected by their CHECK constraints; a fresh signup through the real API still returned ok and wrote a correctly lowercased row with `lang='de'`; all test rows deleted, table empty again. Nothing else changed — Netlify deploy and the draft editorial copy sign-off are still both outstanding and still require the owner.

**Follow-up (third pass, same session) — course card artwork (`components/courses/CourseArtwork.tsx`) rewritten after the owner reviewed the local preview and gave visual feedback on an annotated screenshot.** Two complaints: (1) the "n8n × Pinecone" caption read like an AI-generated watermark; (2) the SVG connector lines did not actually meet the logo tiles — they stopped short or stabbed through, reading as sloppy. Fixes: removed the caption entirely, along with the `[container-type:inline-size]` wrapper that existed only to size it in `cqw`. **Root cause of the disconnected lines, worth keeping as a durable lesson:** the logo tiles are HTML positioned in percentages while the connectors are SVG drawn in a 160×100 viewBox, and the first version hand-wrote both coordinate sets independently — they disagreed by a few units, enough that every line missed its tile. The rewrite makes a single `HUBS`/`CHIPS` constants table the only source of truth, so tile positions and line endpoints are both derived from it — moving a logo and its wiring now follow automatically. Added `edgePoint(a, b, overlap)`, which finds where a line crosses a tile's boundary using a Chebyshev radius (half-size divided by the larger direction component — a plain half-size overshoots past the corner on diagonals) and then pulls the endpoint `overlap` units back inside the tile so the line ends tucked underneath it instead of stopping exactly on the boundary (which left a hairline gap, worse on the rounded corners and on diagonal approaches). Because line ends are now tucked under the tiles, tile backgrounds had to become fully opaque (`bg-surface`, previously `/50` and `/85`) — a translucent fill would show the tucked-in line end and reintroduce the seam. Removed the `float-drift` animation from the chips and its now-unused `@keyframes floatDrift` / `.float-drift` rules from `app/globals.css` — a drifting tile is a tile its connector no longer meets, so the animation was structurally incompatible with exact joins. Repositioned the OpenAI chip to x=140, y=33 in viewBox units: it was boxed in by the card's top-right arrow badge (`CourseCard.tsx`, roughly x≥139, y≤21) on one side and the Pinecone hub's corner (x=127.2, y=32.8) on the other; an intermediate position cleared the badge but left OpenAI and Pinecone almost touching with no visible connector run, so the final position clears both. Noted in the component that the geometry only holds because the scale is uniform — the panel is locked to 16:10 and the viewBox is 160×100, so tiles stay square; changing either ratio would break the join math. **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `/courses` and `/courses/rag-agent-n8n-pinecone` both 200; artwork inspected at 3x zoom on the carousel card (400px), 2x on the detail-page hero (531px), and at mobile width (332px card) — all six connectors (Claude→n8n, Drive→n8n, MCP→Pinecone, OpenAI→Pinecone, Gmail→Pinecone, and the n8n↔Pinecone coral spine) land on their tiles at every size, and the caption is gone. **Status unchanged otherwise:** still nothing deployed to Netlify — owner approval is still required before any push — and the draft editorial copy still awaits sign-off; both remain open below.

**Orchestration + design-output session only — no `execution/` script was written or modified.** Source material: `assets/logo/logo-idea-pics/`, 4 unique JPG photos of hand-drawn logo sketches (2 of the 6 files in that folder are byte-identical duplicates of two of the 4 originals, by file size). **Deliverable:** a Claude Artifact of the "Design" type — an infinite pan/zoom canvas of 10 artboards (`Main` index + palette, `A`–`G` one per concept, `Box` container/colour system, `Scale` favicon ladder / browser tab / site header / app tiles) — at https://claude.ai/artifact/8mWNSEx8rcMjdnu6YGDBWK, titled "XoXoCom Logo Concepts." **Status: private** — the user must open the Share menu on the page before anyone else can view it. Brand tokens used (sourced from `sites/xoxocom/app/globals.css` and `site.config.json`, not changed): canvas `#0b0e11`, ink `#eaecef`, muted `#8c8f92`, surface `#1e2329`, border `#2b3139`, accent Coral `#fb6b4c`, font Manrope.

**Seven concepts vectorised from the sketches**, each shown in 5 treatments (bare coral / coral container / dark container / monochrome ink / reversed-out-of-ink), a wordmark lockup on dark and light, and a 16–52px scale test: A "X-Face Head" (notched bot head, X as the face), B "Twin-Bar Bot" (head + two bar eyes + antennae + legs), C "Starburst X" (heavy X with a 4-point flare at the crossing), D "Star Frame" (open 4-point star holding a small x), E "Mixed Eyes" (bar eye + star eye + small star above), F "Sprinting Bot" (full stride, speed lines), G "Bot to Stardust" (same stride, leading limbs breaking into 4-point stars).

**Three interpretation calls made without the user present, flagged for confirmation, not yet resolved:** (1) Concept A read the sketch's X as the bot's *face* rather than an XoXo monogram inside a container — if the latter was intended, A needs a redraw. (2) Concept G placed the stars on the leading (right) edge per the sketch, so the bot runs *into* dust rather than trailing it — can be flipped if that's wrong. (3) Concept F's pen scribble behind the head was replaced with three clean speed lines, since a scribble does not vectorise cleanly.

**A real constraint surfaced and documented on the artifact itself:** concepts B, F and G do not hold up at 16px — too much internal line detail for a favicon-sized glyph. If either is chosen as the primary mark, it needs a reduced companion glyph for small sizes (same lesson `build_logo_lab.py`'s wordmark option already taught for a different mark, 2026-08-06 — width/detail limits at true tab pixel size, not full-scale review). **CORRECTED, same day, later session (see the 2026-09-20 17:15 entry below): this "B, F, G" list was an estimate made before anything was actually rendered and is wrong. After rendering and inspecting the page, only Concept C (Starburst X) survives 16px untouched; A and D degrade to silhouettes with D's inner x disappearing entirely; B, E, F, and G do not read at 16px at all. Six of seven marks need a reduced companion glyph if chosen, not three. Treat this paragraph as superseded and the later entry as the correct finding.**

**No concept has been chosen.** The artifact is unshared. Nothing was committed to `assets/logo/` from this round.

**Technical learning (self-annealing, environment-level, not a repo script):** the session's scratchpad directory path is itself ~233 characters on this machine, so long artboard filenames (e.g. `A-X-Face-Head.dc.html`) pushed past Windows' 260-char `MAX_PATH` and raised `FileNotFoundError` on write. Fixed by using a short scratchpad subfolder (`xl/`) and single-letter/short filenames, with human-readable names kept only in the canvas index's `title` field. Separately: the Artifact tool's Read-permission check rejects the Windows 8.3 short-path form (`C:\Users\ALBERT~1\...`) — the full long-form path (`C:\Users\Albert Jr Itoumbou\...`) must be passed for both `root` and `file_path`. Neither of these is specific to this repo's code, so no `execution/` or `directives/` change was made for them beyond this log entry.

**Directive check:** `directives/preview_design_systems.md` already documents a "Logo lab" mode (Mode G, §G), but that mode is a different mechanism entirely — `execution/build_logo_lab.py` generates deterministic SVG marks and a self-contained HTML lab page from stroke/overlap geometry parameters. This session used Claude's native Artifact "Design" canvas tool directly, with no execution script, no `--flags`, and hand-vectorised concepts sourced from photographed sketches rather than parametric geometry. Since no script backs this workflow, `preview_design_systems.md` was left unchanged rather than describing a tool that isn't code — see Open Items below for the resulting decision the user should make.

Please try to re-authenticate by running 'netlify logout' and 'netlify login'.` To isolate whether the CLI session or the stored PAT itself was the dead credential, the token in `.env` was tested directly against the Netlify API, independent of the CLI: `curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" -H "Authorization: Bearer $NETLIFY_AUTH_TOKEN" https://api.netlify.com/api/v1/user` returned **401** — the PAT itself is revoked or expired, not just the CLI's local session, so relinking the CLI alone would not have fixed it. The token was 40 chars, `nfp_`-prefixed; all three of the repo's `.env` backups (`.env.bak-20260807`, `.env.bak2-20260807`) held the identical value, evidence the value wasn't accidentally clobbered by an edit — it simply expired, which `nfp_`-prefixed Netlify PATs can do. This is not a code or config regression in `deploy_netlify.py` and none of it was changed in response. **Fix needs the owner**: mint a fresh PAT at `https://app.netlify.com/user/applications#personal-access-tokens` and update `NETLIFY_AUTH_TOKEN` in the repo `.env`, or run `netlify logout` then `netlify login` interactively (never from a non-interactive/agent session — it blocks on a browser handshake). **Blast radius confirmed nil**: the failure is before any `env:set` succeeds and long before `netlify deploy --prod` runs; the live site was reverified serving its unchanged pre-session content afterward. Safe to simply re-run the same deploy command once the credential is refreshed.

**Directive reconciliation (this pass):** `directives/deploy_to_netlify.md` — new Edge Case documenting the misleading `Missing required path variable 'account_id'` error, its real cause (expired auth, not a config problem), the `netlify status` / direct-`curl` diagnosis sequence distinguishing a stale CLI session from a revoked PAT, the fix (owner-only: fresh PAT or interactive `netlify logout`/`netlify login`), and the confirmed-nil blast radius; a matching one-line cross-reference added under Error Handling.

**Open, blocking:** re-run `python execution/deploy_netlify.py --slug xoxocom` once `NETLIFY_AUTH_TOKEN` is refreshed. Everything else for the deploy is ready — site linked (`siteId 5ec12ba7-2b39-4c85-8aa4-d3c8db971b2a`), all other required `.env` vars present and valid, production build verified. This deploy also doubles as the blog's **first-ever** production release — `https://www.xoxocom.net/blog` currently 404s — so the post-deploy check needs a careful click-through of `/blog`, `/admin/login`, and the sitemap's blog entries, not just the usual nav smoke test. The local git working tree still holds all of this session's changes uncommitted.

### 2026-08-08

**XoXoCom — the `/admin` blog authoring surface (auth, post list, editor, topics, image upload) is built and verified end to end against both a dev server and a real production build; the standing "single blocking item" from 2026-08-07 (the `blog` schema not exposed to PostgREST) is now resolved, and all three author accounts are provisioned. NOT DEPLOYED — awaiting operator review of the local preview. ✅ (locally) / ⛔ (deploy).**

1. **Auth layer built: `sites/xoxocom/lib/supabase-auth.ts`.** An anon-key-plus-session Supabase client (`@supabase/ssr`), distinct from the service-role client the public site uses. `getCurrentAuthor()` resolves the signed-in user via `auth.getUser()` (never `getSession()`, which does not verify the JWT) and then checks for a matching row in `blog.authors` — authentication alone is not authorisation. `requireAuthor()` is the same check but throws; every Server Action calls it first, because a Server Action is an independently addressable endpoint that no layout gate protects on its own.

2. **Admin data layer built: `sites/xoxocom/lib/blog-admin.ts`.** Reads `blog.posts` (the table, not the `published_posts` view, since drafts must be visible to their author) through the session client, so RLS applies to every admin query. Nothing is cached. Exposes `StaleWriteError` (an update's `WHERE updated_at = <loaded value>` matched zero rows — someone else saved first) and `SlugTakenError` (a Postgres unique-violation). `setPostStatus` (Publish/Unpublish/Archive/Restore) deliberately skips the `updated_at` check that `updatePostRow` (the form save) enforces — a status click is a decision about the row as it stands, not a save of a possibly-stale copy.

3. **`sites/xoxocom/lib/slugify.ts` built.** Pure, dependency-free. Froze a permanent decision: German umlauts transliterate the German way (`ä→ae`, `ö→oe`, `ü→ue`, `ß→ss`), not the Unicode-normalisation way, because changing this later would silently change what slug a title generates and 404 any already-published URL that got regenerated. `uniqueSlug()` suggests `slug`, `slug-2`, `slug-3`, … against a taken-slugs list; the database's UNIQUE index remains the real arbiter.

4. **`sites/xoxocom/lib/markdown.ts` split.** The Markdown→HTML step moved into a new `lib/markdown-render.ts` with no `server-only` guard and no sanitising, so the admin editor's live preview can import the byte-identical renderer directly on the client without pulling `sanitize-html` (~50 KB) into the browser bundle. `lib/markdown.ts` now imports that module and adds the sanitising allowlist pass on top — the load-bearing security boundary, since a post body is a DB row rendered via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`.

5. **The full `/admin` route tree built.** `app/admin/layout.tsx` (shell + `noindex`, deliberately holds no access check to avoid gating its own login page), `app/admin/login/page.tsx` (zero-client-JS sign-in form; generic error messages so no response confirms which addresses have accounts; `?next=` deep links survive sign-in), `app/admin/actions.ts` (every mutation — `signIn`/`signOut`, `savePost`, `publishPost`/`unpublishPost`/`archivePost`/`restorePost`, `deletePost`, `saveTag`/`removeTag` — each starting with `requireAuthor()`; `safeNext()` re-validates the `?next=` redirect target server-side against an open-redirect attempt), `app/admin/(protected)/layout.tsx` (**the gate** — a server component, not middleware, specifically because Next.js has shipped a middleware-bypass CVE (CVE-2025-29927) in the past), `(protected)/page.tsx` (the post list — every status, drafts first, each row a self-contained `<form>` so the page ships no client JS at all, including the type-the-slug delete confirmation), `(protected)/new/page.tsx` + `(protected)/edit/[id]/page.tsx` (sharing `components/admin/PostEditor.tsx`; the edit route 404s on a malformed id rather than passing it to Postgres), and `(protected)/topics/page.tsx` (fixes a topic's other-language label — a topic created inline while writing a post can only guess one language's label, and that label is visitor-facing on `/blog`'s filter chips).

6. **`components/admin/PostEditor.tsx` built — the only `"use client"` component in the entire blog feature.** Live preview calls `lib/markdown-render.ts` directly, debounced via `useDeferredValue` so typing stays responsive on a long body. The slug field auto-suggests from the title via `uniqueSlug()` until the author edits it by hand, after which it stops overwriting. Client-side guards mirror the server's: publishing needs a body and an excerpt, a cover image needs alt text, and a published post's slug field carries an explicit warning that changing it breaks the live URL.

7. **`app/api/admin/upload/route.ts` built.** The one Route Handler in the admin (every other write is a Server Action) because it accepts a raw multipart file body. Accepts PNG/JPEG/WebP/AVIF; **SVG is rejected outright** (an `.svg` is an XML document that can carry `<script>` and would be stored XSS from the site's own origin). Acceptance is decided by sniffing magic bytes, not the declared `Content-Type` or filename, so a renamed file cannot get through. 5 MB cap. Stores under `posts/<year>/<random UUID>.<ext>` via the service-role client (the `blog-media` bucket has no anon write), discarding the original filename entirely.

8. **`middleware.ts` and `app/layout.tsx` modified.** Middleware is now `async`; for `/admin*` paths only it refreshes the Supabase session cookie (so a short-lived access token doesn't expire mid-edit) — it makes no access decision itself, that is entirely the `(protected)` layout's job. The root layout now drops the public `SiteHeader`/`Footer` chrome for any `/admin*` path, since App Router gives a nested route no way to escape the root layout.

9. **Env/config/scorer wiring completed.** `execution/sync_env_local.py` and `execution/deploy_netlify.py` both gained a third shared-var group (`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`) gated on a new optional `has_blog` flag in `site.config.json`; the server-side Supabase pair (`SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`) is now pushed when **either** `has_contact_form` or `has_blog` is true, since both features share one Supabase project. `has_blog` defaults to false when absent, so every `site.config.json` written before the blog existed keeps generating exactly the `.env.local`/Netlify env it generated before. `lib/config.ts` gained `has_blog?: boolean` on `SiteConfig`. `xoxocom/site.config.json` set `"has_blog": true` and added `"blog"` to `pages`. `score_seo.py` and `score_speed.py` both added `/blog` (index only, never an article slug) to `PUBLIC_ROUTES`.

10. **Schema access resolved: the single blocking item carried over from 2026-08-07 is now closed.** The `blog` schema is confirmed exposed to PostgREST — `PGRST106` no longer occurs, and `/blog` serves the two seeded posts instead of its empty state.

11. **All three author accounts now provisioned, closing an item open since 2026-08-06/07.** `blog.authors` now has three rows: `albert@xoxocom.net` → Albert Jr Itoumbou, `tudor@xoxocom.net` → Tudor Laslau, `info@xoxocom.net` → Patryk Kwitowski (the only one that could write in the prior session). All three accounts are confirmed with passwords set in the Supabase dashboard; none had actually signed in as of this date.

12. **Verification: 61/61 assertions on the dev server, then 61/61 again on a real production build (`next build` + `next start`), driven through the real forms over HTTP** — not through direct database or unit-level calls. Covered: the auth gate (unauthenticated visitor redirected with the deep link preserved), generic error messages with no account enumeration, the `?next=` open-redirect guard, draft invisibility (public URL 404s, absent from `/blog`'s index and from the sitemap), all five server-side validations (title length, slug format, excerpt length, body required to publish, cover requires alt), publish-to-live within the same request cycle (no rebuild), `<script>` and `onerror` stripped from the rendered public page, the stale-write refusal, archive, the type-the-slug delete confirmation, and sign-out. **Plus 6/6 on the upload route**: a valid PNG accepted; an SVG renamed to `.png` refused by the magic-byte sniff; a file declaring `image/svg+xml` refused outright; a file over 5 MB refused; the stored path is randomised (not derived from the original filename); the returned public URL is actually readable.

Fix: added `EXCLUDE_NAME_PREFIXES = (".env",)` and a `fn.startswith(EXCLUDE_NAME_PREFIXES)` check alongside the existing `EXCLUDE_NAMES` filter — same rationale as `.next`: `.env.local` is regenerable at will from the two sources of truth (root `.env` + `site.config.json`). Verified three cases by piping hook input on stdin: `.env.local` alone newer than `progress.md` → exit 0, no block (previously blocked); a real source file newer (`sites/xoxocom/lib/copy.ts`) → still emits a block decision, so the guard was not weakened; `stop_hook_active: true` → still exits 0 silently, so the once-per-turn loop safety is intact. Note for accuracy: the `lib/copy.ts` mtime touch used to prove the third case had no actual content change (`git diff --stat` on it is empty).

It shifts headings down one level so a post body can never emit a second `<h1>`, always emits an `alt` attribute on images (even for `![](url)`), and sanitises the rendered HTML through an allowlist before it reaches `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. 19/19 behavioural tests pass, including a regression test proving the legal pages' `marked` singleton is unaffected.

9. **New code written against the locked directions:**

A new blog-only block was appended underneath, scoped to `.post-prose` alone, covering elements legal notices never use: a larger measure, `scroll-margin-top` on headings (the site header is sticky and `lib/markdown.ts` puts an `id` on every heading, so anchor jumps and in-page TOC links land below the header rather than under it), images, figure/figcaption, blockquote, `pre`, inline code, and tables — tables scroll inside their own container so the page body never scrolls sideways on a wide table.
   - **`sites/xoxocom/lib/blog.ts`** (new) — the data layer for the public site. Public reads go only through the `blog.published_posts` view, never the `posts` table directly, because the service-role client bypasses RLS and reading the base table would risk exposing unpublished drafts. Reads are wrapped in `unstable_cache` keyed on language with a 300-second floor and tag invalidation; `lang` is passed in as a function argument rather than read inside the cache callback, because calling `cookies()` inside an `unstable_cache` callback throws. Markdown is rendered (via `lib/markdown.ts`) inside the post cache itself, so sanitisation runs once per cache-fill rather than once per request. Every read degrades to an empty result instead of throwing, so a Supabase outage blanks the blog instead of 500ing the page. Also exports three pure helpers: `parseTagsParam` (canonicalises and caps `?tags=` at five tags, bounding the crawlable URL surface), `tagHref`, and `formatPostDate` (timezone pinned to Europe/Berlin so Netlify's UTC build runtime and a German editorial desk agree on what "today" is).
   - **Typecheck passes** across the site with both new files in place.

Fix: took the Session pooler connection string from Project Settings → Database (port 5432, username `postgres.<project-ref>`, host `aws-1-eu-central-1.pooler.supabase.com`) and put it in `SUPABASE_DB_URL`. Used the session pooler, not the transaction pooler on port 6543, because the transaction pooler is not reliable for DDL. This only affects the Python migration scripts — the live site reaches Supabase over the REST API using `SUPABASE_URL` + `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`, so a broken `SUPABASE_DB_URL` never affects the live site. A backup of the previous `.env` is at `.env.bak-20260807`; both it and `.env` are gitignored via the `.env` / `.env.*` rules. The change was verified surgical — same seven keys before and after, only the one value plus explanatory comments changed. Discovery detail worth keeping for future sessions: the connection worked at the start of the session and failed later the same day, so this was a live Supabase-side change, not a long-standing misconfiguration.

With the `blog` schema still unexposed in Supabase (Exposed schemas not yet added), the degradation path was exercised for real — 14 `PGRST106` errors logged with clear `[blog] … failed` prefixes while every page still returned 200 and the sitemap still served its static routes.

21. **Defect found this pass, root-caused and fixed later the same session: a soft 404 on `/blog/<unknown-slug>`.** The response initially came back as HTTP **200** with the not-found body instead of a real 404. Verified in both `next dev` and a real production build, so it was not a dev artifact. Genuinely unrouted paths such as `/totally-unknown` returned 404 correctly, so the defect was specific to `notFound()` thrown during render of `/blog/[slug]`. Two fixes were tried at the time and did not help: moving the fetch and the `notFound()` call up into the route segment, and replacing the async not-found boundary with a synchronous one. Mitigation in place at the time: `generateMetadata` returned `robots: { index: false, follow: false }` for a missing post, so search engines were told not to index the dead URL even though the status code was wrong. The working hypothesis at the time — that the root layout's `cookies()`/`headers()` calls were to blame — turned out to be **wrong**; see item 21a below for the real root cause and fix, reached later the same session.

21a. **Root cause found and fixed: `app/blog/loading.tsx` and `app/blog/[slug]/loading.tsx`, both added earlier in this same session, were the actual cause.** Next.js wraps a segment that has a `loading.tsx` — and every child segment beneath it — in a Suspense boundary; once that boundary's fallback starts streaming, the HTTP status is locked at 200, so a `notFound()` thrown later in the same segment tree renders the 404 body under a 200 status. This is documented Next.js behavior (the "Status Codes" section of the `loading.js` docs), not a bug, and has been reported and closed without a fix against `vercel/next.js` repeatedly (issues #45801, #64446, #70447, #76474) as an accepted consequence of streaming. `app/blog/loading.tsx` was the subtle half of it — a `loading.tsx` in `/blog` is inherited by `/blog/[slug]`, so it broke the article route even though it was written for the index. **Fix:** both `loading.tsx` files were deleted. The index skeleton was preserved by wrapping `<BlogIndexContent>` in a `<Suspense>` inside `app/blog/page.tsx`, with the fallback moved into a new `components/blog/BlogIndexSkeleton.tsx` — a `<Suspense>` placed inside a page is scoped to that route only (not inherited), and `/blog` never calls `notFound()`, so nothing is lost there. The article route now has no loading skeleton at all — a deliberate trade, since a correct 404 matters more than a placeholder on a cached page. `app/blog/page.tsx` carries a regression-guard comment stating the same rule. **Verified on the dev server:** `/blog/<unknown>` returns 404; `/blog` returns 200 with exactly one `<h1>` and real content (not stuck on the skeleton); `/blog?tags=x` returns 200; the homepage and legal pages return 200; the sitemap returns 200 with 11 URLs; typecheck is clean. Worth noting for context: even during the window when the soft 404 existed, no dead URL would have been indexed — Next.js automatically injects `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` on any `notFound()` render, on top of the explicit noindex `generateMetadata` already set. Also noted for future reference: if a genuine 404 is ever needed on a route that must keep a `loading.tsx`, the documented workaround is to do the existence check in middleware and return the 404 from there. **The fix was then also confirmed against a real production build** via `execution/autoresearch/serve_prod.py up --slug xoxocom`: `/blog/<unknown-slug>` returned a real 404 with `noindex` present, all twelve public routes (`/`, `/produkte`, `/ueber-uns`, `/kontakt`, `/impressum`, `/datenschutz`, `/agb`, `/leistungen/ai-transformation`, `/blog`, `/blog?tags=x`, `/sitemap.xml`, `/robots.txt`) returned 200, the sitemap had 11 URLs, and `/blog` rendered exactly one `<h1>`. The production server was then stopped with `execution/autoresearch/serve_prod.py down --slug xoxocom`. This item is fully closed — verified on both dev and production, nothing carried into the next session.

22. **`directives/blog_publishing.md` was left unregistered in the SEO/speed scorers on purpose.** `PUBLIC_ROUTES` in `execution/autoresearch/score/score_seo.py` and `score_speed.py` still does not include `/blog`, because the blog copy is still placeholder text and the meta title/description would fail the length checks and drop the currently-passing SEO score. That registration happens once real copy lands.

31. **Second soft-404 finding investigated and accepted this pass — see `directives/blog_publishing.md`'s Edge Cases for the full writeup.** With the HTTP status already fixed to a real 404, further investigation found the custom `not-found.tsx` boundary itself is not server-rendered: the response body for `/blog/<unknown-slug>` is only the layout shell (~53 characters of visible text, no `<h1>`), because the real not-found markup ships only in the RSC flight payload and renders client-side. A genuinely unmatched route (`/totally-unknown`) does server-render Next's own default 404 (~474 characters, with an `<h1>`), and `/blog` itself server-renders correctly (its `<h1>` and empty-state sentence are both present outside any `<script>` tag) — so this is specific to the custom boundary. Four fixes were tried and none changed the behavior: `not-found.tsx` in the parent segment (`app/blog/`); as a sibling of the throwing page (`app/blog/[slug]/`); rewritten as a plain synchronous component with no `cookies()` call; and moving the fetch plus the `notFound()` call up into the route segment. Identical in `next dev` and in a real production build. **Accepted, not fixed:** this is Next.js framework behavior with no application-level fix. The reasoning — the HTTP status is already correct at 404, which is what search engines act on; Next injects `noindex` on any `notFound()` render; Googlebot executes JavaScript and renders the branded 404 normally; the only affected visitors are those with JavaScript disabled plus primitive bots, a small cost on a page nobody should reach. The documented alternative if this ever becomes worth fixing: do the slug-existence check in middleware and return the 404 from there, backed by a cached slug set rather than a full content fetch.

### 2026-08-06 (hook note)

**Stop hook false positive — write-ordering quirk in the documenter's own output, diagnosed and recorded as a self-anneal note. No work was actually missing. 🟡**

The `Stop` hook (`execution/hooks/ensure_progress_logged.py`) blocked session end even though the session's work was already fully logged. Mtime diagnosis: `assets/logo/*.svg` (the actual work) at 16:51:53, `directives/progress.md` at 16:53:40, `directives/preview_design_systems.md` at 16:53:53 — the sibling directive landed 13 seconds *after* the log entry that described it, in the same documenter pass.

**Root cause:** the hook compares mtimes across `sites/`, `execution/`, `assets/`, and `directives/` (excluding `progress.md` itself) against `progress.md`'s own mtime. When a single documenter pass writes `progress.md` first and a sibling directive (e.g. `preview_design_systems.md`) second, that directive is now newer than the log describing it, and the hook trips — even though nothing is actually unlogged.

**Practical rule going forward:** when a documenter pass touches both `progress.md` and another directive in the same pass, write `progress.md` last.

**Possible durable fix — proposed, NOT implemented:** either make the hook ignore other files under `directives/` when comparing, or compare only non-`directives/` work (`sites/`, `execution/`, `assets/`) against `progress.md`'s mtime. This requires the owner's approval before anyone edits `execution/hooks/ensure_progress_logged.py`, per the documenter's write-scope restriction to `directives/` only.
