### 2026-08-06 (later still)

**XoXoCom — logo lab follow-up: a fourth candidate, `wordmark` (Option D), added to `execution/build_logo_lab.py`, plus two bug fixes. Directive updated. Review-only; nothing wired into `sites/xoxocom/`. 🟡**

1. **New candidate: `wordmark`.** The user supplied a reference image of the full "XoXoCom" wordmark — both X's coral (`#fb6b4c`), every other letter ink (`#eaecef`) — matching the rule already recorded in `XOXOCOM_BLUEPRINT.md` §3.2 — and asked for it as a fourth kit option in all three views (monogram/icon/lockup), same as the other three. Unlike `twin`/`interlock`/`fused` (pure stroke geometry), the wordmark is type: its glyphs are outlined from the site's Manrope variable font subset using `fontTools` (`instantiateVariableFont` pinned to `--wordmark-weight`, default 800, matching the header's `font-extrabold`) via `SVGPathPen` + `TransformPen` (y-flip) + `BoundsPen` (tight ink bounding box). Outlining rather than emitting `<text>` is deliberate — the resulting SVG has no font dependency at render time, so it works as a favicon, in an `<img>`, or on a machine without Manrope installed; the output is small (~2.6 KB).

2. **New flags:** `--wordmark-weight` (800), `--tracking` (-0.02 em, matching the header's `tracking-tight`), `--wordmark-ink` (default `#eaecef`, the color of the icon file's non-X letters).

3. **New outputs:** `assets/logo/xoxocom-mark-wordmark.svg` and `assets/logo/xoxocom-icon-wordmark.svg`.

4. **Tinting rule differs from the other three, and matters.** The stroke marks are entirely `currentColor`, so they're tinted with the accent. The wordmark carries the accent on its two X's internally and leaves the rest as `currentColor`, so it must be tinted with the ground's ink color instead — tinting it with the accent would make the non-X letters vanish into a single-color blob. The lab page handles this with a per-mark `tint` role.

5. **Honest finding recorded on the lab page itself:** the wordmark measures ~6.26:1, far too wide to work as a favicon — its letters go sub-pixel well before 16 px. It's included because it's the correct mark for a header, email signature, or share image, not the tab slot; the tab-size comparison at the top of the page demonstrates this directly. Its lockup view also differs from the other three: since the wordmark is already a lockup, the pairing shown is wordmark + legal suffix "UG" rather than mark-plus-name.

6. **New optional, build-time-only dependency**, added to `requirements.txt` with an explanatory comment: `fonttools>=4.50.0` and `brotlicffi>=1.1.0` (brotli lets fontTools decompress the `.woff2`). If either is missing, or `--font` is unreadable, `build_wordmark()` returns `None`: the script prints a warning, skips only the two wordmark asset files, omits Option D from the lab page, and notes the skip in the spec table. Verified this degrades cleanly — it does not crash.

7. **Two bugs found and fixed this round, both verified:**
   - Progress-logging calls used `Path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)` on the output paths, which raised `ValueError` for any relative `--assets-dir`/`--out-dir` (or any path outside the repo). Fixed with a `_rel()` helper that falls back to the full path, plus `.resolve()` on both directories.
   - The icon wrapper (`svg_icon`) previously scaled by width only. Refactored into a generic `icon_wrap()`/`mono_wrap()` pair that fits both axes (`min(avail/cw, avail/ch)`), so a mark taller than it is wide would still be contained. Confirmed byte-identical output for the three pre-existing stroke marks (all wider than tall, so width remains the binding constraint for them) — no regression.

8. **Determinism re-verified** across all variants including the outlined wordmark: same flags in, byte-identical files out.

9. **Deliverable republished at the same URL:** https://claude.ai/code/artifact/19de563e-340c-4739-b892-1e4b27b5b0cf

**Directive reconciliation (documenter, this pass):** `directives/preview_design_systems.md` — updated the Inputs and Tools/Scripts bullets for `execution/build_logo_lab.py` from three to four candidates, describing the wordmark's outlining approach, its distinct tinting rule, its measured aspect ratio, its optional dependency and graceful-skip behavior, and its new flags; updated Process subsection G to flag the wordmark's unsuitability as a favicon when handing the lab to the user; updated the Outputs bullet to list the fourth candidate's files and note they're conditional on `fontTools`/`brotlicffi`; added Edge Cases for the wordmark skip condition, the tinting rule, the aspect-ratio finding, the now-resolved relative-path crash, and the now-fixed icon-fit-both-axes behavior.

### 2026-08-06 (reviewer pass)

**XoXoCom — logo lab: second reviewer sub-agent pass on `execution/build_logo_lab.py`, covering the `wordmark` addition and its two prior bug fixes. 2 HIGH findings, both fixed and verified. Directive updated with new Edge Cases. Review-only; nothing wired into `sites/xoxocom/`. 🟡**

This closes the open item carried from the prior entry: "the wordmark addition and its two bug fixes have not yet been through the reviewer sub-agent."

1. **HIGH — icon-wrapper scale rounded to 2dp, clipping the wordmark.** `icon_wrap` formatted its `scale()` factor through `_n()`, which rounds to 2 decimal places — fine for coordinates, wrong for a multiplier. Fitting the wordmark (~9394 font units wide) into the 100-unit icon square needs scale ≈ 0.00809, which `_n()` rounded to 0.01, a 24% inflation. Because `translate()` was computed from the *true* scale, the wordmark rendered off-centre and overflowed the icon viewBox (ran to x≈105.9 against a 100-unit box) — it would have been visibly clipped in exactly the favicon/tab test the lab exists to run. **Fix:** added a separate precise formatter `_p()` (6dp), used for both `translate()` and `scale()` in `icon_wrap`; `_n()` keeps its 2dp role for coordinates, with both docstrings now stating which is for what. **Verified:** for all four icon variants, every mark is fully inside the 100-unit viewBox and the left/right and top/bottom gaps are equal to within 0.02 units (twin L/R 12.00 T/B 32.24; interlock and fused L/R 12.00 T/B 22.46; wordmark L/R 12.00 T/B 43.93). This also slightly improved the three stroke icons, whose scale had previously been rounded (e.g. 0.4578 → "0.46").

2. **HIGH — unguarded glyph `.draw()` calls could abort the entire script and write zero files.** Only the font-loading step in `build_wordmark` was inside a `try/except`; the two per-glyph layout/draw loops were not. TrueType tables decompile lazily, so a malformed/composite glyph or a degenerate instancer-produced contour surfaces at `.draw()` time rather than at `TTFont()` load time. Since `build_wordmark` runs before the `files` dict is flushed to disk, an exception there aborted `main()` and wrote **no output files at all** — not even the three working stroke marks — contradicting the documented "skips only the two wordmark assets" contract. **Fix:** the entire function body after the imports is now inside one `try/except Exception: return None`. **Verified** with both a truncated and a garbage font file: the wordmark is skipped with a warning and all 7 stroke-mark asset files are still written.

3. **Reviewer explicitly re-verified clean** (no new findings): wordmark width/height match the ink extents; determinism across runs; nonzero fill-rule counters remain holes under the y-flip reflection; `--wordmark-ink` is escaped before reaching the SVG attribute; both wordmark SVGs parse as valid standalone XML with viewBox/title/aria-label; `_rel()` + `.resolve()` correctly handle out-of-repo directories.

4. **Deliverable republished at the same URL** after re-verifying determinism: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/19de563e-340c-4739-b892-1e4b27b5b0cf

**Directive reconciliation (documenter, this pass):** `directives/preview_design_systems.md` — added two Edge Cases to the logo lab section: the now-fixed icon-scale rounding bug (precise vs. coordinate formatting) and the now-fixed unguarded-glyph-draw crash risk, both stated as resolved-and-verified so a future reader knows the current behavior, not just the history.

### 2026-08-06 (later)

**XoXoCom — a third design-review round: three provisional favicon/tab-mark candidates via a new script, `execution/build_logo_lab.py`. Reviewer sub-agent findings fixed. Directive documented. Origami-provenance open items reconciled. Review-only; nothing wired into `sites/xoxocom/`. 🟡**

1. **New execution script:** `execution/build_logo_lab.py` (std-lib only, deterministic — same flags in, byte-identical files out). Generates three provisional favicon/tab-mark candidates, all built from one shared X primitive on a 100-unit cap square, so a mark can be judged before it is wired in as the favicon:
   - `twin` — the current wordmark reduced to its two accent-bearing X glyphs, side by side (aspect ~2.14:1; wide, so it shrinks hard in a square icon).
   - `interlock` — the two X's overlapped and woven. The pair crosses at exactly two points (the other strand pairs run parallel); each X passes over the other at one crossing, making the figure invariant under a 180° rotation about the overlap centre — so "neither X overshadows the other" is a property of the geometry, not a judgement call. Aspect ~1.38:1.
   - `fused` — same overlap, painted rather than woven: both X's translucent (default stroke-opacity 0.72) so the shared region reads brighter. Nothing is cut and nothing stacks, and with no hairline gap it is the variant that survives 16 px intact.

   Outputs: `assets/logo/xoxocom-mark-{twin,interlock,fused}.svg` (monograms in `currentColor`, one file per tint), `assets/logo/xoxocom-icon-{twin,interlock,fused}.svg` (square app/tab icons with explicit fills, since a favicon inherits no CSS), `assets/logo/xoxocom-interlace-proof.svg` (annotated crossing diagram), `.tmp/logo_lab/index.html` (standalone review page), `.tmp/logo_lab/artifact.html` (body-only fragment for publishing). Flags: `--stroke` (0.20), `--overlap` (0.62), `--gap` (0.14), `--clearance` (0.26), `--fused-opacity` (0.72), `--linecap` (round), `--icon-pad` (0.12), `--icon-radius` (0.22), `--icon-bg`, `--accent`, `--assets-dir`, `--out-dir`, `--font`.

2. **Design/engineering decisions captured as durable knowledge** (now recorded in `directives/preview_design_systems.md`'s Mode G and its Edge Cases/Error Handling):
   - The site's real Manrope typeface is inlined as a base64 data URI from the Next build cache (`sites/xoxocom/.next/static/media/4c9affa5bc8f420e-s.p.woff2`), because the Artifact publish target's CSP blocks font CDNs; if that file is missing, the page falls back to a system stack with an on-page note rather than failing.
   - `--overlap` below roughly 0.50 makes the interlace geometrically impossible — the crossings crowd the X arm tips and the gap overruns the strand end. The script's `cut()` helper raises a `ValueError` naming the fix (raise `--overlap`, or lower `--stroke`/`--clearance`) rather than emitting a malformed mark. This caught a real bug during development: the original 0.34 default produced a reversed, negative-length segment.
   - `interlace_report()` validates `0 < --overlap < 1.0`; at or above one cap square the two X's coincide or invert (this previously caused a `ZeroDivisionError` in the icon scaling — now fixed, see reviewer findings below).
   - Crossings are keyed by *which X reads as over* (`over_a`/`over_b`), not by y-value, because SVG y grows downward and "low/high" inverts the visual meaning.
   - `--accent` and `--icon-bg` are interpolated into SVG/HTML attributes and the generated SVG is inlined verbatim into the lab page, so both are escaped via `html.escape(quote=True)` before use, to prevent attribute break-out/markup injection.
   - Page output is forced to pure ASCII (non-ASCII characters become numeric character references), because the published page's `<head>` is supplied by the Artifact wrapper and the file can't declare its own charset.
   - Nothing is wired into `sites/xoxocom/`. Adopting a mark means copying the chosen icon file to `sites/xoxocom/app/icon.svg`, which Next serves as the favicon automatically.

3. **Reviewer sub-agent run; 4 MEDIUM findings, no HIGH, all fixed and verified:**
   - On-page prose had left/right and upper/lower swapped versus the interlace diagram — corrected.
   - `--accent` did not reach the on-page monogram/lockup/proof tints (only the icon files respected it) — now threaded through consistently.
   - Unescaped `--accent`/`--icon-bg` allowed attribute injection into the generated SVG/HTML — now passed through `html.escape(quote=True)`.
   - Unbounded `--overlap` crashed the `fused` variant with a `ZeroDivisionError` — `interlace_report()` now validates the range and raises a clear `ValueError` instead.

4. **Deliverable published for review:** https://claude.ai/code/artifact/19de563e-340c-4739-b892-1e4b27b5b0cf

1. **New execution script:** `execution/build_origami_lab.py` (std-lib only, deterministic — same flags in, byte-identical files out). It generates ten translucent folded-paper SVG shape assets into `assets/origami/`: `origami-disc.svg`, `origami-hex-flower.svg`, `origami-triangle.svg`, `origami-twist-square.svg`, `origami-twist-hex.svg`, `origami-gem.svg`, `origami-pleat.svg`, `origami-chevron.svg`, `origami-shards.svg`, `origami-crane.svg`. It also builds a preview "origami lab" page at `.tmp/origami_lab/index.html` (standalone) plus `.tmp/origami_lab/artifact.html` (body-only fragment for publishing as a Claude artifact), showing the shape family in both brand tints, an opacity ladder, a two-tint overlap demo, and three in-situ hero mock-ups on real hero copy with the real scrim/glow. Flags: `--assets-dir`, `--out`, `--fill-min` (default 0.05), `--fill-max` (0.24), `--crease` (0.30), `--edge` (0.62).

2. **Design decisions captured as durable knowledge** (now recorded in `directives/preview_design_systems.md`'s Mode E and its Edge Cases):
   - Every shape is painted entirely in `currentColor` with per-facet `fill-opacity`, so one asset file serves both brand tints — `color: #fb6b4c` (coral accent) or `color: #ffffff` (white accent). No duplicate per-tint files.
   - Translucency is baked into the geometry per facet (0.05–0.24 range), not applied as a wrapper opacity; overlapping facets accumulate, producing the folded-paper depth.
   - Shapes must be corner- or side-weighted in the hero: the hero's existing radial scrim swallows anything placed directly behind the headline; a centred shape is effectively invisible.
   - When animating a centred layer, use the standalone CSS `translate` property rather than `transform: translate(...)`, because keyframes that animate `transform` clobber a centring transform.

### 2026-07-17 20:15

**XoXoCom — diagnosed and fixed why the OG share preview still looked German/small after the English translation deploy; root cause was stale immutable cache, not a code defect. Two fixes committed and deployed. Directive updated with the caching lesson. ✅ (server-side is fully correct; residual WhatsApp per-device preview lag is expected and outside our control.)**

Follow-on to the 19:30 entry (OG image + tagline translated to English, commit `c818d32`): the user reported the share preview still rendered German, and that the WhatsApp preview differed by device — English on desktop, still German on mobile — and showed the image as a small thumbnail rather than the large banner.

**Diagnosis.** A cache-busted fetch plus direct inspection of the live PNG confirmed the origin was already serving the correct English OG image — the German the user saw was a stale cache, not a regression. Root cause: `sites/xoxocom/app/opengraph-image.tsx` (Next's file-based `next/og` route) is served at a stable URL (`/opengraph-image`, no hash) with `Cache-Control: public, immutable, max-age=31536000`. A year-long immutable cache keyed on an unchanging URL means browsers, the Netlify CDN edge, and social-media scrapers (WhatsApp, etc.) that had already fetched the old German image never see the new one — the content change never propagates to anyone who already cached it.

**Fix 1 — commit `cfad68d`** ("fix(xoxocom): version the OG image URL to bust stale immutable caches"): changed `OG_IMAGE` in `sites/xoxocom/lib/seo.ts` from `"/opengraph-image"` to `"/opengraph-image?v=2"`, with a doc comment explaining the token must be bumped whenever the image's rendered content changes. `OG_IMAGE` feeds both `buildMetadata()` (every main page's OG/Twitter images) and `app/layout.tsx`'s default OG/Twitter images, so this one constant change covers the whole site. Verified locally and live: `og:image` is now `https://www.xoxocom.net/opengraph-image?v=2`, returns 200 `image/png` (English content), SEO scorer 100/passed with `og_image_renders` green. Deployed, deployId `6a5a113578e0009681250c`.

**Fix 2 — commit `02b0d92`** ("fix(xoxocom): declare og:image:type for reliable large link previews"): added `type: "image/png"` to the `openGraph.images` entry in both `lib/seo.ts`'s `buildMetadata()` and `app/layout.tsx`'s default OG images, so rendered HTML now includes `<meta property="og:image:type" content="image/png">`. This strengthens the large-format-preview signal for scrapers that sometimes fall back to a small thumbnail without it (matches the WhatsApp desktop small-thumbnail symptom reported). `tsc --noEmit` clean. Verified live on main and legal pages: `og:image:type=image/png` present alongside the already-correct `og:image:width=1200` / `og:image:height=630` / `twitter:card=summary_large_image`. SEO 100/passed. Deployed, deployId `6a5a7e3143be8e068db17eca`.

**Caveat recorded, not a code issue:** the remaining "German on WhatsApp mobile" report is WhatsApp's own per-device link-preview cache, keyed per shared page URL, refreshing independently per client — there is no server-side purge for it. It self-clears over days, or immediately if the shared link carries a new query string (forcing WhatsApp to treat it as a new URL), or via a forced re-scrape through the Facebook Sharing Debugger. The server-side OG output is now fully correct and versioned; this residual device-level lag is expected and outside the site's control.

**Directive reconciliation (documenter sub-agent, this pass):** `directives/auto_optimize_seo.md` — added a new Edge Case, "OG image content changed but the old version keeps showing up," documenting the immutable-cache/stable-URL mechanism, the requirement to bump the `?v=` token in `OG_IMAGE` (`lib/seo.ts`) whenever `app/opengraph-image.tsx`'s rendered content changes, the fact that the scorer's `og_image_renders` check cannot detect a stale-but-still-valid cached image (so this step is manual, not caught by scoring), and the WhatsApp-specific per-device cache caveat with its three clearing paths.

**Git state:** commits `cfad68d` and `02b0d92` are on branch `doe`, **not yet pushed to the git remote** (join `af065aa` / `c818d32` / `503f5e2` / `61c5aea` as unpushed — now five unpushed commits on `doe`). Both were committed with an explicit pathspec to avoid re-bundling the separate `lm_landing_pages`/matfit changes staged in this shared git index, which remain staged and untouched.

**Open items:** add `cfad68d` and `02b0d92` to the unpushed-commits tracking. No new script or process changes beyond the two `lib/seo.ts`/`app/layout.tsx` edits described above.

### 2026-07-17 18:00

**XoXoCom — default-language flip hardened, both AutoResearch experiments re-validated, and the site deployed to production. Same-day continuation of the 00:00 flip. ✅**

**1. Re-ran the SEO AutoResearch scorer's language assumption.** `execution/autoresearch/score/score_seo.py`'s `PUBLIC_ROUTES` fixture hardcoded an expected `<html lang>` per route — all `"de"`. Since a cookieless crawler now gets English on the 7 main routes after the morning's flip, `html_lang_correct` would have failed on all of them. Updated `PUBLIC_ROUTES` so the 7 main routes expect `"en"` and the 3 legal routes (`/impressum`, `/datenschutz`, `/agb`) still expect `"de"`; updated the module docstring and the `PUBLIC_ROUTES` comment to explain why (the map reflects what a crawler actually sees, not a scoring trick).

**2. Fixed a real defect the flip exposed: legal pages were losing their German `<html lang>`.** The root layout set `<html lang>` purely from the language cookie, so a cookieless visitor on `/impressum` etc. would now get `lang="en"` around still-German content — the exact body/lang mismatch the flip was meant to eliminate elsewhere. Fix, server-side, while keeping the surrounding chrome (nav/footer) in whatever language the visitor chose:
   - New `sites/xoxocom/middleware.ts` — sets an `x-pathname` request header so the root layout (which Next.js doesn't hand the current pathname to directly) can tell which route is being rendered. Matcher excludes `_next/static`, `_next/image`, `favicon.ico`, `robots.txt`, `sitemap.xml`, `opengraph-image`.
   - `lib/copy.ts` — added a `LEGAL_ROUTES` constant and an `isLegalPath(pathname)` helper (plain server-safe module, no `"use client"`).
   - `app/layout.tsx` — now also reads `headers()`. Computes `chromeLang` from the cookie (for the visible nav/footer and `LanguageProvider`) and a separate `htmlLang` (`isLegalPath(pathname) ? "de" : chromeLang`) for the actual `<html lang>` attribute.
   - `lib/i18n.tsx` — now reads `usePathname()`; the single `useEffect` that used to both set `document.documentElement.lang` and write the cookie/`router.refresh()` was split into two: one sets `document.documentElement.lang` on `[lang, pathname]` changes; the other writes the cookie and calls `router.refresh()` only on `[lang, router]` changes (guarded by a mounted ref). This means a plain client-side route change (pathname changes, language doesn't) no longer triggers a `router.refresh()` — preserving the speed-experiment's client-navigation win.
   - `app/impressum/page.tsx`, `app/datenschutz/page.tsx`, `app/agb/page.tsx` — changed `export const dynamic = "force-static"` to `"force-dynamic"`, because a statically-built page has no per-request `x-pathname` header to read.

**3. Reviewer sub-agent run on all of the above; two HIGH findings, both fixed:**
   - The 7 main pages' `<title>`/description metadata were still hardcoded German while the body and `<html lang>` were now English — exactly the crawler-visible inconsistency the flip was supposed to remove, and one the scorer's length/uniqueness checks can't catch (it doesn't check language). Fixed by translating all 7 main pages' title + description to English (`app/page.tsx`, `app/produkte/page.tsx`, `app/ueber-uns/page.tsx`, `app/kontakt/page.tsx`, `app/leistungen/{ai-transformation,expert-consulting,business-coaching}/page.tsx`), keeping every string inside the scorer's length bands (titles 30–60 chars, descriptions 120–160 chars).
   - The 3 legal pages were emitting `og:locale=en_US` (inherited from the site-wide flip) despite being permanently German. Fixed by adding optional `locale`/`alternateLocale` parameters to `buildMetadata()` in `lib/seo.ts` (default unchanged — the site-wide English-primary pair); the 3 legal pages now pass `locale: "de_DE"`, `alternateLocale: "en_US"` so an alternate locale tag is still present (keeping the scorer's `og_locale_alternate` check passing everywhere) while the primary OG locale on those pages is correctly German.
   - A MEDIUM finding (force-dynamic legal pages reading `content/*.md` at request time inside the Netlify function) was flagged as "verify on a real deploy" — confirmed fine post-deploy (all 3 legal routes return 200 with German content live). A LOW finding (a route-groups alternative to middleware) was noted but not pursued.

**4. Both AutoResearch experiments re-validated.** SEO: 100.0/passed on local dev and on the live production site (https://www.xoxocom.net) post-deploy; `tsc --noEmit` clean. Speed: built the production bundle via `serve_prod.py up` (port 3100, built outside OneDrive) — scored 87.56/passed with the embedded SEO gate at 100.0 (frozen baseline 87.43 from `results/speed.tsv`; the +0.13 is English-vs-German copy byte drift, not a code change to the speed experiment). Legal pages rendered `lang="de"`, main pages `lang="en"` in the prod build. `serve_prod.py down` afterward.

**5. Committed:** branch `doe`, commit `af065aa` ("feat(xoxocom): make English the default site language") — 18 files, 147 insertions / 62 deletions, includes the new `middleware.ts`. An earlier commit attempt (`6bd3e4c`) had a mangled subject line from a shell-quoting mistake and was corrected via `git commit --amend`, so `af065aa` is the final commit. Deliberately excluded from this commit: unrelated `../lm_landing_pages/*` working-tree changes (separate project) and the still-untracked `WHY_WE_APPEAR_ON_GOOGLE.md` / `execution/autoresearch/EXPLAINER.md` (both carried-forward items from prior sessions).

**6. Deployed to production** via the documented OneDrive external-build workaround (`deploy_to_netlify.md` Error Handling): killed all stray `next dev`/`next-server` process trees with `taskkill /F /T` first; robocopied `sites/xoxocom` → `C:\xoxo-build\xoxocom` (`/MIR`, excluding `node_modules`/`.next`/`.netlify`); `npm install`; `netlify link --id 5ec12ba7-2b39-4c85-8aa4-d3c8db971b2a`; exported `NETLIFY_AUTH_TOKEN` from the repo `.env`; `netlify deploy --prod`. Clean build in 2m12s, "Deploy is live!", exit 0, deployId `6a5a215825bb545a348600be`.

**New operational lesson, folded into `deploy_to_netlify.md`'s Error Handling section:** robocopy must be run from PowerShell (or with MSYS path-conversion disabled), not from the Git Bash tool — Git Bash's MSYS layer auto-converts flags like `/MIR` into fake Windows paths (`C:/Program Files/Git/MIR`), producing a robocopy usage-error exit 16 instead of a copy. Also, robocopy's `/XD` exclude list breaks on the OneDrive conflict-copy directory name `.next (1)` (space + parens); excluding plain `.next` (as already documented) is sufficient. Robocopy's own success exit codes are 0–7 (3 = files copied + extras removed under `/MIR`), so callers must not treat every non-zero exit as a failure.

**7. Verified live on https://www.xoxocom.net:** all 12 routes (10 pages + `/sitemap.xml` + `/robots.txt`) return 200; `/` cookieless = `lang="en"` + English title "XoXoCom UG — Modern Ways of Working Meet A.I."; `/` with an `xoxocom-lang=de` cookie = `lang="de"` + German nav (toggle still works both directions); `/impressum` = `lang="de"` + German content, and **stays** `lang="de"` even with an EN cookie (the middleware pin works); the SEO scorer against the live URL returns 100.0/passed.

**Directive reconciliation performed:** `directives/deploy_to_netlify.md` — added the robocopy-from-PowerShell / MSYS-path-conversion caveat, the `.next (1)` exclude caveat, and the robocopy 0–7 exit-code note to the OneDrive external-build workaround (Error Handling section); also noted the external build copy can be left in place between deploys and switched the documented example command from `/E` to `/MIR` to match. Checked `auto_optimize_seo.md` and `auto_optimize_speed.md` for stale "German default" wording — neither directive documents a per-route language default or the scorer's `PUBLIC_ROUTES` lang expectations in prose, so no edit was needed in either; the scorer code change (point 1 above) is self-contained to the script and its own docstring/comments, which are the actual source of truth the directive already points readers to.

### 2026-07-09 12:00

**XoXoCom — indexation milestone confirmed: "XOXOCOM" search on Google now surfaces www.xoxocom.net. Beginner-friendly explainer `WHY_WE_APPEAR_ON_GOOGLE.md` written. GSC "Request Indexing" item closed. ✅**

The user confirmed that a Google search for "XOXOCOM" now surfaces `www.xoxocom.net` in results — it did not before the SEO/GSC/Bing work landed. This validates the crawl → index → rank pipeline built and verified across the 2026-06-18 through 2026-06-26 sessions (SEO completeness 32.57→100, GSC verification, sitemap submission, Bing Webmaster Tools import) end to end: the site is now both indexed and discoverable by name.

New deliverable at the repo root: `WHY_WE_APPEAR_ON_GOOGLE.md` — a plain-language, explain-like-I'm-10 document (distinct from `execution/autoresearch/EXPLAINER.md`, which is the technical/harness explainer) that walks through why the site now appears on Google and maps each already-completed step to its actual role in the pipeline:

- SEO completeness (33→100) = made the site crawlable and readable by search engines, and controls how the result looks once shown (title/description snippet, OG image) — it does not by itself cause indexing.
- The live custom domain = a prerequisite (nothing to index without it).
- Google Search Console verification + sitemap submission + Request Indexing = the actual decisive step that got the site into Google's index.
- Bing Webmaster Tools setup (GSC import, 10 URLs) = indexing on Bing, DuckDuckGo, Yahoo, and AI-search engines that pull from Bing's index — a separate index from Google's.
- The speed experiment (Lighthouse 86.13→87.43 / 92→96) = a ranking tie-breaker among indexed pages, not the reason the site appears at all.

### 2026-07-08 17:00

**AutoResearch Experiment #2 (Speed) — CLOSED. Live Lighthouse bookends captured (92→96), optimized build deployed to production, a mid-deploy OneDrive-corruption incident diagnosed and fixed via the documented external-build workaround. ✅**

**1. Baseline Lighthouse bookend captured (unblocking last session's carryover).** The keyless PageSpeed Insights API quota was exhausted again this session (as it was the prior day), so the bookend was captured via local Lighthouse instead. `npx lighthouse`'s own Chrome launcher (chrome-launcher) crashed on this machine with "Session closed" errors when spawning its own Chrome instance — a new environment quirk. Workaround: launch Chrome manually with `--remote-debugging-port=<port>`, then run `npx lighthouse <url> --port=<port>`, pointing Lighthouse at the already-running browser instead of letting it spawn one. Captured against the live pre-deploy site (https://www.xoxocom.net, commit `718b515`): **92/100** mobile performance — LCP 1.6s, TBT 160ms, CLS 0.125, FCP 1.5s, Speed Index 3.4s.

**2. Deployed the speed-experiment changes (commit `943a165`, byte score 87.43) to production** via `python execution/deploy_netlify.py --slug xoxocom` — a routine "subsequent deploy" per `deploy_to_netlify.md` (no `site.config.json` or site-creation changes needed).

**3. Incident: homepage 500 immediately after a Netlify-reported-successful deploy.** Netlify reported build state "ready" with no error, but `/` returned 500 in production while all 9 other routes returned 200. Root cause: the documented OneDrive build-corruption pattern (`deploy_to_netlify.md` Edge Cases/Error Handling), not a code bug — the identical code had already built and served correctly via `serve_prod.py` (outside OneDrive) earlier. Investigation found several stray Windows `node.exe` processes still running inside the OneDrive-synced `sites/xoxocom` directory and actively writing to `.next`: a leftover `next dev` process from earlier scorer testing, plus orphaned `netlify deploy --prod` / `next build` children (with 15 `jest-worker` grandchildren) left over from an aborted retry-deploy attempt. **New lesson:** killing only the parent PID on Windows does not cascade-kill its children — `netlify deploy` and `next build` child/grandchild trees survive a plain `Stop-Process` on the parent and keep writing to `.next`, which is exactly the corruption trigger the directive already warns about. Use `taskkill /F /T /PID <pid>` (the `/T` flag kills the whole process tree) instead.

**4. Fix applied: the directive's existing OneDrive external-build workaround, run manually.** After killing all stray processes, robocopied `sites/xoxocom` to a fresh `C:\xoxo-build\xoxocom` (`/MIR`, excluding `node_modules`/`.next`/`.netlify`), ran `npm install`, `netlify link --id 5ec12ba7-2b39-4c85-8aa4-d3c8db971b2a`, exported `NETLIFY_AUTH_TOKEN` from the repo `.env`, and ran `netlify deploy --prod` from that copy. Clean deploy — all 10 routes + `/sitemap.xml` + `/robots.txt` now return 200 on https://www.xoxocom.net. The temporary `C:\xoxo-build\xoxocom` copy was deleted afterward (disposable, not committed).

**5. Post-deploy verification.** All 10 public routes return 200. `score_seo.py --base-url https://www.xoxocom.net` still returns 100/passed — confirms the speed experiment did not regress the SEO-100 experiment on the live site. **6. After Lighthouse bookend captured** the same way (second Chrome instance, second remote-debugging port): **96/100** mobile performance (+4) — LCP 1.2s (-0.4s), FCP 1.1s (-0.4s), Speed Index 2.5s (-0.9s), CLS 0 (-0.125), TBT 230ms (+70ms, within normal Lighthouse run-to-run noise). New directive `directives/auto_optimize_speed.md` (commit `c4fa2ea`) documents the full Mode A/B loop, the `serve_prod.py` port range (3100–3199, kept separate from `optimize.py`'s 3000–3099 dev range so a speed run never collides with a SEO/dev loop), and why Lighthouse/PSI is used only as a before/after bookend rather than the loop's keep/revert signal (Lighthouse's ±2–5 point run-to-run variance would corrupt the gate; the byte-budget scorer is deterministic).

**Five iterations logged in `results/speed.tsv`, all committed on branch `doe`:**

| Iter | Score | Decision | Commit | Hypothesis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 86.13 | kept (baseline) | `718b515` | Frozen baseline, prod build via `serve_prod.py` |
| 2 | 86.17 | kept | `c43bf87` | Lazy-load the 3 canvas heroes via `next/dynamic` (`ssr:false`) |
| 3 | 86.54 | kept | `c4fa2ea` | Server-side i18n for `/ueber-uns` only (`getCopy` cookie helper; provider `router.refresh()` on toggle) |
| 4 | 86.52 | **reverted** | `511e4b0` | Server-side i18n for `/produkte` alone — proved per-page conversion can't cross the shared-bundle valley |
| 5 | 87.43 | kept | `511e4b0` | Full server-side i18n: `COPY` leaves the client bundle entirely |

The winning refactor (iteration 5) moves the bilingual `COPY` tree off the client JS bundle for good: content components now read language server-side via a new `lib/server-copy.ts` `getCopy()` helper; `SiteHeader`, `LangToggle`, and `ContactForm` receive translated strings as props instead of calling `useLang()` directly; `LanguageProvider` was slimmed down to language state plus `router.refresh()` on toggle; a new `components/LazyHero.tsx` wraps the lazy-mounted hero canvases from iteration 2. Per-route first-load JS dropped from 149–158 KB gz to 145–146 KB gz. Verified manually in the browser: DE↔EN toggle works across header, nav, contact form, and footer; lazy heroes render; form labels localize correctly.

**Calibration finding** (recorded in `program/speed.md`): the Manrope variable font makes weight-list reduction a byte no-op — `next/font` serves the same unicode-range slice regardless of the declared `weight` list, and the scorer only counts preloaded/`.p.` "critical" fonts, not every slice referenced in CSS. Do not retry that lever.

### 2026-06-26 00:00

**XoXoCom — production domain connected, TLS provisioned, SEO infrastructure deployed; site live at https://www.xoxocom.net. ✅**

**1. Custom domain connected and TLS provisioned.**

Pre-session reality check revealed the Netlify site had the apex `xoxocom.net` set as primary domain, `www` was not added, DNS was external (no Netlify DNS zone), the apex A-record pointed to a non-Netlify IP (2.57.91.91), and the `www` CNAME already pointed to Netlify but had no SSL cert (broken HTTPS).

DNS ordering lesson (to be folded into `deploy_to_netlify.md`): Netlify rejected setting `www` as primary via API (422 Unprocessable Entity) until the apex A-record pointed at Netlify's load balancer. Netlify cannot verify domain ownership until DNS resolves to Netlify. The correct sequence when an external registrar controls DNS is: (1) update apex A-record → 75.2.60.5 and www CNAME → `<site>.netlify.app` at the registrar; (2) wait for propagation; (3) only then call the Netlify API to set the primary domain and provision TLS.

The user updated the apex A-record at their registrar to 75.2.60.5. Propagation was verified on Google and Cloudflare resolvers. Via Netlify API: `custom_domain` set to `www.xoxocom.net`, `domain_aliases` set to `[xoxocom.net]`; TLS cert provisioned (issued, covers both hosts, expires 2026-09-27). Verified: https://www.xoxocom.net → 200; https://xoxocom.net → 301 → www; cert valid. No code change was needed — `site.config.json` `site_url` was already `https://www.xoxocom.net` and is the single source of truth that feeds `SITE_URL` in `lib/config.ts`, which in turn drives `metadataBase`, `robots.ts`, `sitemap.ts`, `seo.ts`, and JSON-LD throughout the site.

**2. SEO infrastructure deployed (was committed but never live).**

Discovered the SEO-completeness work (commits 264e865 and 6ea5568) had never been deployed — the live site was a stale June-18 manual CLI deploy. `/robots.txt`, `/sitemap.xml`, and `/opengraph-image` all returned 404; the homepage had no canonical, OG, or JSON-LD tags in production HTML.

- `/sitemap.xml` — 200; all public route URLs use `www.xoxocom.net`.
- `/opengraph-image` — 200 (branded OG image renders).
- Homepage HTML — canonical `https://www.xoxocom.net`; `og:url` `https://www.xoxocom.net`; JSON-LD Organization present.

Production URL: https://www.xoxocom.net.

### 2026-06-18 20:00

**XoXoCom — AutoResearch SEO harness built and run; SEO score 32.57 → 100.0; directive `auto_optimize_seo.md` created. ✅**

**AutoResearch harness (new files under `execution/autoresearch/`):**

- `execution/autoresearch/score/score_seo.py` — the fixed scorer ("prepare.py"). Pure Python stdlib (urllib + html.parser). Crawls all 10 public routes' rendered HTML plus `/sitemap.xml` and `/robots.txt` on a running Next server and prints one JSON line: `{"score":0-100, "passed":bool, "subscores":{...}, "routes":{...}}`. Deterministic: same server HTML → same number. `passed=false` is a hard gate (triggers revert regardless of score). Per-route checks = 70% of score (unique title 30–60 chars, unique description 120–160 chars, single h1, canonical on configured domain, html lang, full Open Graph set including og:locale + og:locale:alternate, Twitter card, JSON-LD, img alt). Site-level = 30% (valid sitemap listing all public routes, robots referencing the sitemap, OG image URL actually returning an image — not just a present meta tag). The og:image-renders check was added after the earlier "100" was identified as a false pass (the OG image route was crashing but still emitting a meta tag).

- `execution/autoresearch/optimize.py` — the loop driver. Subcommands: `state --metric seo [--base-url]` (prints research direction + results history + live weak-spot hints) and `evaluate --metric seo --hypothesis "..." [--serve] [--auto-git]`. Mode A (default): recommends KEEP or REVERT; agent commits deliberately. Mode B (`--auto-git`): commits on improvement / `git reset --hard` on regression (requires clean tree; use for unattended runs). `--serve` starts and stops `next dev` automatically, picks a free port in the 3000–3099 range.

- `execution/autoresearch/program/seo.md` — the human-owned research direction. Lists what may be edited, what is off-limits, scoring rules summary, and a prioritized hypothesis list. Not edited by scripts.

- `execution/autoresearch/results/seo.tsv` — append-only experiment log. Gitignored.

**Site-level SEO changes (`sites/xoxocom/`):**

- `site.config.json` gained `site_url` (`https://www.xoxocom.net`); `lib/config.ts` exposes `SITE_URL` (overridable via `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` env var).
- New `lib/seo.ts` — `buildMetadata({title, description, path})` factory (canonical + full Open Graph incl. og:locale/og:locale:alternate + Twitter card + og:image) and `organizationJsonLd()`.
- New `app/sitemap.ts`, `app/robots.ts`, `app/opengraph-image.tsx` (branded OG image via next/og).
- `app/layout.tsx` — metadataBase, default OG/Twitter, JSON-LD Organization, Google Search Console verification slot (env `GOOGLE_SITE_VERIFICATION`).
- Every `app/**/page.tsx` uses `buildMetadata` with unique titles/descriptions in the ideal length bands.

**Experiment log (4 iterations):**

| Iter | Score | Decision | Hypothesis |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 32.57 | kept (baseline) | Current site, pre-optimization |
| 2 | 88.64 | kept | sitemap.ts + robots.ts + metadataBase + OG + JSON-LD + per-page metadata |
| 3 | 100.0 | kept | Explicit og:image on every route via buildMetadata |
| 4 | 100.0 | kept (re-confirmed) | Fix crashing OG image (Satori multi-child) + harden scorer to verify og:image renders |

**New directive:** `directives/auto_optimize_seo.md` created with all required sections (Goal, Inputs, Tools/Scripts, Process, Outputs, Edge Cases, Error Handling). Documents Mode A and Mode B runs, the `state` and `evaluate` subcommands with all flags, how to read `results/seo.tsv`, the GSC/measurement post-score steps, and the future-phases extension pattern.

**Open follow-ups added (see Open/Unfinished Items below):**

- Connect canonical domain `www.xoxocom.net` to Netlify; set `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` env var.
- Create Google Search Console property, verify (set `GOOGLE_SITE_VERIFICATION` env var in Netlify), submit sitemap.
- True per-language hreflang needs locale-routed URLs — a future phase requiring user sign-off.

### 2026-06-18 10:00

**XoXoCom — bilingual performance bugfix: client-side navigation + cookie-driven SSR. 🟡 (local preview only; not committed or deployed)**

Root cause: every internal link was a plain `<a href>`, forcing a full-page hard reload on each navigation. Each reload rebooted React and reset `LanguageProvider` to its German default, painted German, then re-read the `localStorage` preference and re-rendered to English (the visible flash); in dev the reload also recompiled the route (the 2–3s lag).

Two-part fix:

1. **New `sites/xoxocom/components/SmartLink.tsx`** — renders a Next.js `<Link>` for internal routes (paths starting with `/`) and a plain `<a>` for external URLs, hash anchors (`#…`), and `mailto:` links. All props pass through. Converted all internal links across `SiteHeader`, `FooterLinks`, `ContactForm`, and all seven content components (`HomeContent`, `BusinessCoachingContent`, `AiTransformationContent`, `ExpertConsultingContent`, `ProdukteContent`, `UeberUnsContent`, `KontaktContent`) to use `SmartLink`. Hash and external links intentionally remain plain `<a>`. Result: internal navigation is now client-side; the in-memory `LanguageProvider` state survives the route change — no reload, no re-translate, no flash.

2. **Cookie-based language preference + server-side rendering** — Language preference moved from `localStorage` to a cookie (`xoxocom-lang`). `app/layout.tsx` is now an async server component: reads the cookie via `next/headers` `cookies()`, sets `<html lang>` accordingly, and passes `initialLang` to `LanguageProvider`. The provider initializes its state from `initialLang` (no post-mount read); on change it writes the cookie. Result: even a hard refresh renders the saved language on the server with zero flash.

   **Lesson recorded — shared constants between server and client modules:** the `LANG_COOKIE` constant was initially defined in `lib/i18n.tsx`, which carries `"use client"`. Importing a plain constant from a client module into a server component yields a client-reference stub (a function), not the string value — so `cookies().get(LANG_COOKIE)` silently returned `undefined` and SSR stayed German. Fix: shared server-readable constants must be defined in a non-client module. `LANG_COOKIE` was moved to `lib/copy.ts` (no `"use client"` directive); both the server layout and the client provider import it from there.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` exits 0; `curl` with no cookie returns `lang="de"` + German "Vorname"/"Nachricht absenden"; `curl -H "Cookie: xoxocom-lang=en"` returns `lang="en"` + English "First name"/"Send message"; browser test confirms clicking a nav link performs client-side navigation (~0.8s, no full reload, window marker survived, language stayed English, destination rendered English immediately). Note: reading `cookies()` opts the affected routes into dynamic rendering — acceptable for this site and Netlify's Next.js runtime.

**Git state:** fix is on branch `doe` (uncommitted), incorporated into the bilingual feature. Not deployed to Netlify.

During the bilingual conversion, `components/content/HomeContent.tsx` and `lib/copy.ts` were written with the "Unsere Leistungen / Services" section rebuilt from an older in-memory copy rather than from the actual committed file. This clobbered customizations present in `app/page.tsx` at commit 6fe8c0b ("home content refinements").

Recovered via `git show HEAD:doe/full_web_design/sites/xoxocom/app/page.tsx` and restored into `lib/copy.ts` (both `de` and `en` trees) and `HomeContent.tsx`:

1. **Card order corrected** — A.I. Transformation (left) → Projekteinsätze (middle) → Business Coaching (right). The earlier refactor had reshuffled these.
2. **Card bodies restored to short teasers** — German text is the user's exact wording from commit 6fe8c0b; English entries are new matching teasers. The accidental draft had replaced them with full-copy paragraphs.
3. **Per-card roles `<ul>` list removed** — The `roles` data field was dropped from the home Leistungen entries in `lib/copy.ts`; the `<ul>`/`<li>` rendering block was deleted from `HomeContent.tsx`. The home section is now teaser-only, matching the original page design.

**Lesson recorded:** when refactoring an existing page whose content may have been hand-customized, read the full current file AND run `git show HEAD:<path>` to check for committed-vs-working drift before overwriting. Never rebuild content from an in-memory copy.

**Git state:** fix is incorporated into the uncommitted bilingual feature on branch `doe`. Not deployed.

New `sites/xoxocom/lib/copy.ts` — bilingual content dictionary. `de` is the source of truth; `en` is typed `typeof de`, which forces the English tree to mirror the German one at compile time (missing or renamed key = TypeScript error). Holds all user-facing copy: nav items, CTAs, header aria-labels, footer, contact form, and all seven pages (home, business-coaching, ai-transformation, expert-consulting, produkte, ueber-uns, kontakt).

3. New `sites/xoxocom/components/LangToggle.tsx` — segmented "DE | EN" switch. Rendered in the header desktop bar and in the mobile menu.

Legal pages (Impressum / AGB / Datenschutz) intentionally stay German for legal validity; only the footer legal-nav aria-label localizes.

Content components consume `useLang()`. `app/layout.tsx` wraps everything in `<LanguageProvider>`. `SiteHeader`, `Footer` (promoted to client component), `FooterLinks` (gained an `ariaLabel` prop), and `ContactForm` all read copy from the context. Nav items, CTA text, `NavItem`, and `NavChild` config were removed from `lib/config.ts` and moved into `lib/copy.ts`; `config.ts` still exports `site` and `SOCIALS`.

   - Otherwise compares mtimes: if any file under `sites/`, `execution/`, `assets/`, or `directives/` (excluding `directives/progress.md` itself, and pruning `node_modules`/`.next`/`.netlify`/`__pycache__`/`.git`/`.tmp`/the `hooks` directory) is newer than `progress.md`, it prints `{"decision":"block","reason":"..."}` instructing the model to call the documenter sub-agent before ending. **XoXoCom — Business Coaching hero built and deployed; `record_canvas_animation.py` created and production-tested; hero recording made mandatory before every deploy; OneDrive deploy workaround documented. ✅**

1. New component `sites/xoxocom/components/HeroGraphCluster.tsx` (sibling of `HeroGraph.tsx`):
   - Canvas animation visualising the graph-theory **local clustering coefficient**. A focal node lights up; radial spokes draw to its k neighbours; triangles among those neighbours close and fill in Coral; a small arc-gauge sweeps to C = 2·(links among neighbours)/(k·(k−1)). Uses a random-geometric graph so triangles and clustering are always visible. Focal node rotates left-to-right through ~5 high-clustering nodes per cycle.
   - Same technical pattern as the other two heroes: reads CSS design tokens at runtime, honours `prefers-reduced-motion`, responsive via `ResizeObserver` + `devicePixelRatio`, subtle pointer parallax.

2. Integrated into `sites/xoxocom/app/leistungen/business-coaching/page.tsx`:
   - Canvas mounted as absolute background layer with the same centre text-protection scrim and accent glow pattern used by the other service pages.

3. New execution script `execution/record_canvas_animation.py` (reviewer-approved, end-to-end tested):
   - Records any on-page `<canvas>` animation to `assets/exploded-views/<name>.webm` (VP9) + `.mp4` (H.264/yuv420p/faststart, even dims enforced via crop filter).
   - Drives `browser-harness` via a piped Python snippet. In-browser: CDP `Page.startScreencast` prevents rAF throttling; composites each transparent canvas frame onto the `--color-bg` background token in an offscreen canvas; captures with `MediaRecorder` (VP9); reads webm back base64-chunked; python side writes to disk and calls ffmpeg.
   - Flags: `--url` (required); `--name`/`--out` (mutually exclusive, required); `--selector` (default `canvas`); `--seconds` (default 12); `--fps` (default 30); `--bitrate` (default 8M); `--bg` (default: reads `--color-bg` token from the live page); `--settle` (default 2.5); `--no-mp4`.
   - Records at the canvas's native on-page resolution. Reviewer fixes applied: UTF-8 decoding of harness stdout; chunk-scaled subprocess timeout; closes only the tab it opened (no tab leak); polls for `MediaRecorder.onstop` instead of a fixed sleep; substitutes user-supplied tokens last to prevent injection collisions.

4. Hero recording made a mandatory pre-deploy step:
   - `design_website.md` step 9 (Record hero animation) and `deploy_to_netlify.md` step 2 (first-deploy checklist) already document this rule.
   - Asset recorded: `assets/exploded-views/hero-cluster-coefficient.webm` + `.mp4`.

5. Deployed to Netlify production and verified live (HTTP 200, animation confirmed): https://xoxocom-ug.netlify.app/leistungen/business-coaching.
   - Deploy used the external-copy fallback (see below) — not in-place via `deploy_netlify.py`.

6. OneDrive external-build workaround documented in `deploy_to_netlify.md`:
   - Edge Cases section: describes the three failure symptoms (ENOENT during "Collecting page data", `<Html>` import error on `/500`/`/_error`, `lstat ENOENT` during plugin `onBuild` standalone copy).
   - Error Handling section: step-by-step external-copy procedure (kill `next dev`; robocopy site to non-OneDrive path excluding `node_modules`/`.next`/`.netlify`; `npm install`; `netlify link --id <siteId>`; `netlify deploy --prod`). Notes that `deploy_netlify.py` does NOT yet automate this.

**Data-integrity feature — prospect de-duplication (unique index + upsert) implemented, run in production, verified live. ✅**

1. New execution script `execution/add_prospects_dedup_constraint.py`:
   - Collapses existing duplicates in the shared `leads.prospects` table, keeping the most recent row per `(campaign_slug, email)`, then adds a UNIQUE index `ux_prospects_campaign_slug_email` on `(campaign_slug, email)`.
   - Idempotent and safe to re-run; runs dedupe + index in a single transaction. Connects directly via `SUPABASE_DB_URL` (DDL privileges; the service key can't run DDL). Shared with the `lm_landing_pages` workspace (same table). Run once: `python execution/add_prospects_dedup_constraint.py`.
   - Uniqueness is per campaign: the same email under a different `campaign_slug` stays a separate row, and NULL-slug legacy rows are left untouched (NULLS DISTINCT).

2. Submit routes switched from plain insert to upsert:
   - `sites/_template/app/api/submit/route.ts` and the `xoxocom` route now `upsert(row, { onConflict: "campaign_slug,email", ignoreDuplicates: true })`. A repeat submission from the same email on the same site is a no-op at the row level (no duplicate row); the contact email still forwards on every submit.
   - The sibling `lm_landing_pages` routes (`agentic-workflows-101` + `_template`) were switched to the same upsert so the new global unique index doesn't break them.

3. Migration already run in production: 6 duplicate rows removed, index created, verified live (double-submit → 1 row).

4. `execution/deploy_netlify.py` now masks secret env values (`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`, `GMAIL_APP_PASSWORD`) when echoing `netlify env:set` commands.

5. Directives updated to match: `capture_contact_submission.md` (insert → upsert in Process, new dedup script in Tools/Scripts, Outputs and Edge Cases reworked — the stale "No de-dupe" case replaced); `deploy_to_netlify.md` (secret-masking note + new edge case for missing unique index).

No directive code touched (documenter scope). New open item added: swap testing Gmail creds → owner creds before real production. Existing legal follow-ups carried forward.

### 2026-06-16 15:10

**Process improvement — progress-log maintenance codified in CLAUDE.md.**

1. `CLAUDE.md` updated with Operating Principle 4: Keep the progress log current.
   - Specifies `directives/progress.md` must be updated via the documenter sub-agent in three situations: a step completed but needing follow-up (🟡), work left unfinished or blocked (⛔), and end of a working session (✅).
   - Requires every entry to be timestamped and placed at the top of the log (reverse-chronological). Current Status and Open/Unfinished Items must be refreshed each time.
   - A matching bullet was added to the "Key rules" sub-agent trigger list alongside the existing documenter trigger.

2. Effect: future sessions will update `progress.md` without requiring an explicit instruction each time. This closes the process gap where progress was recorded ad-hoc rather than systematically.

No new open items. No open items resolved (this was a process-gap closure, not a content task).

- `build_accent_lab.py` — Present in `execution/`; no directive references it yet.

- [ ] Repo-root `.env` has **no trailing newline**, so appending a variable with `printf 'KEY=v\n' >> .env` silently merges it into the last comment line and dotenv never reads it. Owner's file, owner's call — but adding one newline removes a completely silent failure mode.
- [ ] Any newly scaffolded site inherits **placeholder** legal text from `sites/_template/content/`. It must be replaced with that operator's own details (directives/add_legal_pages.md) before the site goes live, and the jurisdiction confirmed first — the Impressum/Datenschutz pair is German-law specific. A site that collects personal data (a contact form counts) must never ship with placeholders.
- [ ] Next.js gotcha worth remembering: a route folder whose name starts with `_` is a **private folder** and is silently excluded from routing — the build still succeeds and the archive still grows, but the route 404s. Read the build log's route table to confirm a new route actually exists rather than inferring it from a green deploy.
